# AUTODEDUP — autonomous cross-portal listing deduplication

**Program document. This file is the program's source of truth.**
Status: design accepted; W0 census **closed**, W0b in flight; **the entire program runs in shadow mode** (decide, store, write nothing).
Date: 2026-09-16. Rulings D1–D10 below were made by the program lead (operator delegated) on 2026-09-16 and are **binding — they supersede the design text wherever the two differ**.

---

## 0. What this is, and what it must never touch

An **autonomous** engine that finds the same real-world property across nine Czech portals and across time, and decides whether to merge it — at a precision that would make an unattended merge stream safe.

It lives in its own package `autodedup/`, its own Postgres schema `autodedup`, its own Actions lane `autodedup.yml`, its own concurrency group `autodedup`, its own settings namespace `ad_*`.

**What it shares with the rest of the platform, in this program (D4 — the whole trial is shadow):**

- **reads** `listings`, `properties` (for the E4 property-grain guard and the cluster `property_id` stamp), `listing_location`, `listing_snapshots`, `images` (+`phash`), `image_clip_embeddings`, `image_clip_tags`, broker identities — read-only, no DDL on any of them (D8);
- **writes** exactly one shared thing: three new `llm_calls.called_for` values — `autodedup_judge_text`, `autodedup_judge_vision`, `autodedup_judge_gold` — migrated and applied *before* any calling code. **No other shared table is written by this program.**

Everything else in §11 (the merge chokepoint call, the `property_merge_events.generation` post-stamp, the `dirty_properties` enqueue) is **designed here and exercised nowhere in this program**. It is the write path a *later* operator decision may switch on; see Appendix A.

It **never** reads or writes `dedup_sim.*`, never dispatches a `new-dedup-*` lane, never touches the parallel operator-guided program's settings.

### Clean slate is total (D7)

Per CLAUDE.md rule 15 and ruling D7, the engine reads **nothing** from any prior dedup effort: no golden pairs, no `dedup_pair_audit`, no frozen LLM verdict caches, **no `property_merge_events` rows — not as labels, not as must-not-link seeds, not as evaluation truth, not even as counts**. There is no unmerge/split history in this engine's input set at any grain. Ground truth is built fresh, from the judge and the operator, inside `autodedup.*`. `property_merge_events` appears in this document in exactly one role: as the target of a *future* write (E40), never as a read.

### The thesis, in one paragraph

Every expensive signal in this problem is already paid for. `images.phash` (dHash, 64-bit) exists corpus-wide; `image_clip_embeddings` holds ~11.3M vectors; `listing_snapshots` holds the whole price path; `listing_location` holds graded geo; broker identities resolve nightly. The engine's job is to **spend an LLM only where the free signals genuinely cannot decide** — and the width of that uncertain band is not a hope, it is a settings-capped dial that auto-tightens until the month's bill fits. The LLM is the **label oracle**, not the runtime decision engine: a stdlib logistic regression trained on judge labels does the scoring, so cost scales with *new listings*, not with corpus size. No GPU, ever, in steady state.

### The shape, in one sentence

The only primitive is `resolve(conn, listing_id)`. The trial and (beyond this program) the always-on worker lane are that one function in a loop — so there is no second code path to calibrate, and no divergence between what was measured in the trial and what would run later.

---

## 1. Rule index — the engine in one page

Every rule is stated **once**, here or in the section that owns it, and referenced by number everywhere else. **Never renumber E1–E111** — the numbers are cited by code, tests and the other program docs. A rule that changes meaning gets a new number and the old one is marked superseded.
Every rule is stated **once**, here or in the section that owns it, and referenced by number everywhere else. **Never renumber E1–E111** — the numbers are cited by code, tests and the other program docs. A rule that changes meaning gets a new number and the old one is marked superseded.
Every rule is stated **once**, here or in the section that owns it, and referenced by number everywhere else. **Never renumber E1–E111** — the numbers are cited by code, tests and the other program docs. A rule that changes meaning gets a new number and the old one is marked superseded.

**Identity and guards**
- **E1** A duplicate is the *same physical unit* (dwelling, parcel, commercial space) under the same deal type. Not the same building, not the same project, not the same floor plan.
- **E2** **Guard G1 — `category_type` equality.** Both known and unequal ⇒ never a pair, never a merge. NULL = *unknown*, not a conflict. Verbatim from `toolkit/dedup_candidates.py` `categories_compatible`; re-enforced at the chokepoint (`toolkit/property_identity.py`, the `FOR UPDATE` block: `if s_ct is not None and r_ct is not None and s_ct != r_ct: raise MergeError`).
- **E3** **Guard G2 — `category_main_compatible`** (`toolkit/room_taxonomy.py`): compatible when equal, when either is NULL, or when the unordered pair is `{dum, komercni}` — *the one sanctioned cross-type, irrespective of subtype*. So `byt` never merges with `dum`, `komercni`, `pozemek` or `ostatni`; `pronajem` never merges with `prodej`/`drazba`.
- **E4** Guards are evaluated at **property grain as well as listing grain**. The chokepoint reads `properties.category_type` / `properties.category_main` (rolled up from the representative listing), so two listings can pass a listing-grain guard while their properties carry different rolled-up values. The engine evaluates the guard on the same rows the chokepoint would, or it manufactures merges that the last rail refuses.
- **E5** **Merge-grade attribute guards**, deliberately tighter than the parallel program's *candidate* tolerances (whose ±20% area and ±2 floor gate blocking, not a merge): both areas known and differing >8% ⇒ hard reject, 3–8% ⇒ band only, ≤3% ⇒ supporting. Both dispositions known and unequal (non-`pozemek`) ⇒ hard reject. Both `byt` floors known and |Δ| ≥ 2 ⇒ hard reject; Δ = 1 ⇒ band only (portals disagree on whether *přízemí* is 0 or 1).
- **E6** A re-listing after a gap **is** a duplicate. There is no temporal veto anywhere in the engine. `gap_days` is a feature with a learned weight, never a penalty.
- **E7** A same-portal re-post by the same broker **is** a duplicate. `same_source` is a feature, never a veto.
- **E8** Scope is **all-time**. No `is_active` filter in blocking, and **no retention window** on the fingerprint store — a 24-month window would structurally make pre-2024 re-listings unretrievable, which is exactly the class E6 exists to capture.
- **E9** **Catalog-photo subtraction.** A dHash appearing on ≥ `ad_catalog_df` distinct listings *corpus-wide* is marketing stock. It is removed from the evidence set **before any image feature is computed**; its share survives as `catalog_ratio`, a negative feature. Default 8, measured in W2. Corpus-wide, not per-block, because a developer catalogue spans blocks.
- **E10** **Image evidence is three numbers, not one**: `interior_match_ratio`, `exterior_match_ratio`, `plan_match_ratio`, split by `image_clip_tags` family (`toolkit/room_taxonomy` ROOM_FAMILIES: interior / exterior / common / plan / other). A shared site plan or facade is a *building* signal and must never be readable as a *unit* signal. Degrades gracefully when tag coverage is thin (the plan arm goes to zero) instead of leaking into one blended score.
- **E11** **Evidence-diversity precondition — a hard gate, not a weight.** An auto-merge requires corroboration from **≥ 2 of 5 independent families**: IMG, TXT, LOC, BRK, ATTR. *Images alone can never merge a pair.* A hard gate survives a mis-weighted model; developer projects are precisely where the model will be mis-weighted.
- **E12** **Missing data is never a mismatch.** Every feature is a `(value, present)` pair and the model consumes `value·present` **and** `present` separately, so absence learns its own weight. Any other rule erases bazos (zero area on all 138,997 rows), remax, and realitymix's 20,722 attribute-less rows.

**Blocking**
- **E13** The only primitive is `resolve(conn, listing_id)`. Trial and (later) worker lane are that function in a loop.
- **E14** Blocking is **index probes over one precomputed row per listing** (`autodedup.listing_fp`), never a set-based corpus join.
- **E15** Six probes, unioned, each optional, none required: **K1** block+attribute (log-band), **K2** address, **K3** broker+price decile, **K4** dHash bands *(location-free)*, **K5** text SimHash bands *(location-free)*, **K6** foreign.
- **E16** **No probe keys on a coordinate.** Distance is a feature only, masked unless both pins are street-grain or finer, normalised by the two uncertainty radii, and discounted by pin population.
- **E17** Hard caps: ≤ `ad_max_candidates_per_listing` (60) per listing, filled in priority order so the cap can only discard the weakest evidence class; any `(probe, key)` over `ad_max_block_size` (200) is **exploded** — never expanded, and membership becomes a negative feature.

**Features and scoring**
- **E18** ~45 features across 7 families are catalogued (§5), all computed in Python from one bulk fetch per block; zero marginal cost, since every input already exists. **Per the program lead's scope ruling, W2 ships a core set of ~25 and the set grows only by error analysis** — a feature earns its place by fixing an observed error class, not by being cheap.
- **E19** **Price-path EVENT matching**: the fraction of the smaller side's price-*change* events matched by the other within ±3 days and ±1% of Δ. Cross-posted adverts drop price together; two different units do not. Free from `listing_snapshots`, and almost nothing in this repo uses it.
- **E20** Text is split into **template similarity** (tfidf / jaccard / simhash) and **unit-specific similarity** (`rare_token_overlap` over tokens with in-block document frequency ≤ 2, plus numeric-fact overlap). The *gap between the two* is what separates a broker's reused template from the same flat re-posted.
- **E21** Scoring is a **stdlib logistic regression**, calibrated (ECE ≤ 0.05 on a 10-bin reliability diagram, isotonic over bins if not), artifact stored as JSON in `autodedup.models`, activated by a partial unique index — the `tag_head_models` idiom. Stdlib inference, so the same scorer can later run inside `scraper/realtime_worker.py` with no numpy and no sklearn (CLAUDE.md rule 7). A linear model is also *legible*: the validation UI shows per-feature contributions, so the operator can see why a merge was proposed.
- **E22** **Three zones**: `p ≥ T_hi` auto-merge; `T_lo < p < T_hi` LLM judge; `p ≤ T_lo` auto-reject. Below `ad_store_floor` (0.02) nothing is persisted, only counted. In this program "auto-merge" means *auto-merge-decided and stored*, never applied (D4).
- **E23** **Thresholds are per stratum** (D10). A stratum that cannot reach its precision bar gets **no auto-merge band at all** and ships propose-only into the residual view. This is how bazos's weakness becomes visible recall loss instead of invisible precision loss. Made operative in W4c by **E48**.
- **E24** **Certificates** short-circuit to auto-merge, because their precision is structural rather than learned: **K-A** exact `ruian_adm_kod` ∧ equal disposition ∧ area ≤2% ∧ equal floor; **K-B** same source ∧ same broker ∧ text containment ≥0.90 ∧ area ≤1% ∧ **zero active overlap**; **K-C** ≥4 non-catalog exact dHash matches ∧ gallery order preserved ∧ area ≤3% ∧ equal disposition ∧ `catalog_ratio` ≤0.2. All three are retained by ruling. All still pass E2–E5 and E11. (The *disjoint windows* clause in K-B is load-bearing: two simultaneously active listings on one portal by one broker with near-identical text is the developer signature, not a re-post.)
  - **K-A is SUPERSEDED as an auto-merge certificate (W4c).** Measured on the gold holdout it merged at **52.55 % HT precision (n=98)** against K-B's 100 % (n=94) and K-C's 97.6 % (n=150): one RUIAN point plus disposition plus area certifies a *building*, which is the developer-unit false-merge shape itself. `settings.certificate_ka_enabled` defaults **false** and both K-A strata are propose-only under E48; the code path stays so an evaluation can switch it back on. K-B and K-C stand — and now pass **E45/E46/E47** and their **E48** stratum switch before they may merge, so "short-circuit to auto-merge" reads: short-circuit past the *score*, never past the gates.
- **E25** **Band width `b` IS the budget dial.** `monthly = L × c × b × $/pair`. `T_lo` auto-raises until `b` fits `ad_band_budget`, and the **sacrificed recall is printed in every run summary** rather than hidden.

**Merge-zone gates (W4c, measured on the W3 gold holdout)**
- **E45** **Unit-evidence gate.** No pair enters the merge zone — certificate or score — without at least one **unit-specific** corroboration: matched non-catalogue **interior** frames on both sides (`interior_match_ratio ≥ 0.5` with `n_images_min ≥ 1`), the **K-B re-post shape** (same source ∧ same broker ∧ containment ≥ 0.90 ∧ zero overlap), a **shared unit number** at one address point and floor, **a shared agency order code (E60, W8)**, or **≥2 rare shared tokens**. Building-grade agreement — one address, one catalogue, one template, one project name — is not evidence about a unit; it is the developer false-merge shape. Evidence: K-A alone 52.6 % (n=98), K-A *with* the interior arm 88.6 % (n=35), the K-B shape 100 % (n=94).
- **E46** **Developer-signature guard.** Two adverts that were live **together** (`overlap_days > 0`) whose only image agreement is catalogue material (`catalog_ratio_max ≥ 0.80`, interior below E45's bar) are banded. **Not scoped to one portal or one broker** — the same shape appears across two portals (83202 × 10515714, 83467 × 10515837, both gold `same_building_different_unit`); the same/one-broker clauses are tighteners kept as dials.
- **E47** **Developer co-live guard.** One broker, one portal, both adverts live side by side for **more than 30 days**, and no shared unit number ⇒ band, **certificate or not**. Two units of one project photographed together defeat every image and text rule (21000 × 27781 merged as K-C at `interior_match_ratio` 1.0 after 61 co-live days), so the co-live window itself is the separator; a re-post under E6/E7 does not run beside its predecessor for weeks. Swept 0/3/7/14/30/60/90 days: precision flat at 98.06 %, recall rising with the bar, so the default sits at the wide end that still clears the observed case.
- **E48** **The per-stratum merge switch** — D10/E23 made operative. `settings.t_hi_by_stratum`, keyed `<layer>|<side>` exactly as `evaluate.decide_stratum` spells it (`K-C|same`, `model|cross`): **NULL = propose-only** (nothing in that stratum auto-merges, certificate included), a number = that stratum's own `T_hi`, an absent key = the global one. Certificates are read *before* `T_hi`, so this is the only mechanism that can hold one back, and it is why `K-C|same` (94.7 % dev / 85.7 % sealed, under the 0.97 floor) can ship propose-only while `K-C|cross` (100 %, n=127) keeps merging. An unknown side reads as `cross` — a gate fails towards the stricter cell.

**Judge** *(full spec in §6 and the separate JUDGE SPEC)*
- **E26** Two tiers, one forced tool: T1 text (`gpt-5.6-luna`, batched), T2 vision (`gpt-5-mini`, batched, 4+4 images at 768 px q80, `max_tokens=4096`).
- **E27** **Four-way verdict**: `same_property | different_property | same_building_different_unit | insufficient_evidence`. `unit_discriminator` is **required** unless `same_property`. `same_building_different_unit` writes a permanent `must_not_link` row.
- **E28** **PII never crosses the wire**: no broker name, phone or email — only the derived `same_broker` / `same_firm` booleans — and descriptions are scrubbed of Czech contact patterns before truncation.
- **E29** A pair is judged **once**, cached on `(lo, hi, judge_version, tier)`; re-judged only when a *judged field* changed.
- **E53** **The vision judge's photographs are PAIRED BY ROOM (`j2`).** The 4+4 budget is spent on rooms *both* galleries photographed — floor plan, kitchen, bathroom, living room, bedroom, facade, then any other shared room, most-alike first — and `A-n` / `B-n` are presented adjacent under one instruction that says a differing plan or kitchen between paired frames is strong evidence of a **different unit**, while a matching facade, garden, common staircase, site plan or catalogue frame is evidence about the **BUILDING only**. Inside a room the CLOSEST frames are chosen (CLIP cosine, pHash Hamming as tie-break), because the pair a judge has to separate is the most confusable one. `paired` is DERIVED from the frames by `paired_prefix`, never promised by a caller, so the prompt cannot claim a pairing that is not there; a tight pHash match across two different room tags keeps a slot at the END; an untagged gallery falls back to the j1 cross-room CLIP pair. E9/E10 are unchanged and still run first, so catalogue stock is stripped before any pairing. `JUDGE_VERSION = j2`; E29 caches on it, so every j1 verdict stands, and the lane's `gold_version=` argument lets a j2 pass draw exactly the pairs the j1 gold labels live under — **the label belongs to the pair, not to the prompt**. This is the operator's kitchen-to-kitchen instruction, and it is the ONLY place room-matched similarity is allowed to act (D14). (The number E49 was proposed once for a plot-contradiction guard, refused by D13, and has since been spent on the UI's `same_project_different_unit` verdict — so this rule takes the next free number after E52.)
- **E30** Every call goes through `LLMClient.call` so it lands one `llm_calls` row. The `called_for` migration and the `PRICES` rows ship **before** the calling code.
- **E31** Every paid lane carries a pre-flight `--max-usd`, **and asserts it read "done", never only "drew"** — a cap priced against the pre-flight *estimate* has silently refused five whole batches in this repo, spending nothing and reporting nothing wrong. Spend gates per D2: **<$10/run autonomous, $25 hard cap per run, $200 total program**.
- **E32** Cost is read from `llm_calls` after the first pass, never extrapolated from token prices again.

**Clustering**
- **E33** **Constrained union-find with must-not-link**, edges processed in descending confidence so the outcome is reproducible from the edge set alone. Every union validates the **merged cluster**, not the edge. Plain connected components is rejected: one bad edge inside a development would merge the whole project.
- **E34** Cluster invariants: single `category_type`; one compat class; area spread ≤ `ad_cluster_area_spread` (0.08); ≤1 non-NULL disposition for non-land; `byt` floor spread 0; size ≤ `ad_max_cluster_size`.
- **E35** `ad_max_cluster_size` is **measured in W0, not inherited**. The "max 3 children" figure everyone quotes came from a system that *structurally forbade same-source merges* over five portals; E6/E7 invalidate it as a prior. Provisional 8, pending the census.
- **E36** Cluster identity = the smallest `listings.id` ever admitted, immutable for the cluster's life. A new listing **appends** (attaching to the medoid). A cluster is **never split automatically**.
- **E37** **Bridging two existing clusters is outside the autonomous envelope.** The bridge edge is recorded and surfaced; nothing is applied. One wrong bridge would corrupt two properties at once, and there is no data to calibrate a bridging premium.

**Write path — designed here, exercised nowhere in this program (D4). See Appendix A.**
- **E38** The only write would be `merge_properties(source='auto', merge_group_id=<engine uuid>)` — **one retired property per group**, so `unmerge_group` reverses it exactly and reverting a whole generation is linear and conflict-free.
- **E39** `autodedup_write_enabled` is read **fresh at the top of every merge attempt**, never cached across a pass. **Shadow mode — decide, store, write nothing — is the default and the only mode for this entire program**; switching it is a separate operator decision, not a deliverable here.
- **E40** `generation = 'autodedup_v1'` would be **post-stamped** onto `property_merge_events` (`merge_properties` doesn't write it). The seam is non-atomic: the engine records `applied_merge_group` on the pair row **before** calling, and a reconciler re-stamps anything it owns that is unstamped. The engine reads **no row of `property_merge_events` it did not itself create** — the reconciler's predicate is scoped to its own `merge_group_id`s — and nothing read there is ever an input to a decision (D7: no prior merge, unmerge or split history as seeds, labels, truth or counts).
- **E41** **MergeError policy.** A category or property-state refusal is recorded as **terminal** on the pair and never retried. A transient DB error retries ≤3× and then **poisons** the queue row with a reason. A wedged drain is a known failure shape in this repo and must not be invented again.
- **E42** A **merges-per-hour latch** (default 50) and a merges-per-run cap stop a storm; both **latch** — writes stay off until the operator clears them.
- **E43** A **physical-plausibility monitor** runs over every applied merge's spliced price series (simultaneous overlapping prices differing >20%; a step with no market move) as a free, continuous, **label-free precision alarm**. Nobody has to judge anything for it to fire. In shadow mode it runs over *proposed* splices and is a pure metric.
- **E44** **Operator state moves with the merge and the engine says so** (D5 adopted). `merge_properties` runs `carry_operator_state_on_merge` and `reconcile_pipeline_on_merge` inside its transaction (CLAUDE.md rules 16/18/22): pipeline cards move, collections/tags/notes re-point, `notification_dispatches` rows re-point onto survivors. Every applied merge reports what moved; in shadow mode every *proposed* merge reports what **would** move, and that report is shown in the validation UI.
- **E49** **`same_project_different_unit` is a NEGATIVE verdict** (migration 532). A fifth operator verdict for the shape the four could not say: a different **building of the same development project**. Like `different` and `same_building_different_unit` it writes a permanent `autodedup.must_not_link` — an operator who has established that two adverts are different units must never be shown that pair as a merge proposal again. `same_building_different_unit` is unchanged and keeps its own meaning (one building, two units); the two are not folded together, because the developer-project rails (§2) are calibrated against the distinction. The number **E49 was once proposed for a plot-contradiction hard reject and refused by D13** — nothing cites it, so it is free.
- **E50** **A stored split is read back off the pair verdicts, never kept only on the page.** The split IS its pair rows — members ruled `same` are one unit, a negative pair verdict is the relation between two units — so the queue ships `member_verdicts` (every operator verdict on a pair of that cluster's members, *including the pairs the engine never scored*) and the card derives the assignment from them. Without it the ruling is write-only: after a reload every unit select reads "A" over a group the operator partitioned last week, and the next save re-derives all pairs as one unit and **retracts the permanent must-not-links the first save wrote** — silently, with nothing on any surface to show it.
- **E51** **The relation is per UNIT PAIR.** A group regularly holds two units of one *building* and a third advert from a different building of the same *development*. One relation for the whole split records a building those adverts do not share, or throws away the building the other two do — as permanent must-not-links **and** as the negative-control labels the developer rails (§2) are calibrated against, so the one distinction E49 exists to preserve is the one a single value corrupts. `relations:[{unit_a, unit_b, relation}]` names each pair; `relation` is the fill for the rest. The **cluster** row carries the *weakest* relation the split used (`different` < `same_project_different_unit` < `same_building_different_unit`) — the only statement true of the whole group — and the note spells out the pairs when they differ.
- **E52** **Taking back a veto is a deliberate act, at both grains.** A cluster verdict of `same` now retracts the operator's own `must_not_link` on **every pair of the group**: "these are all one property" and a standing veto between two of them is a contradiction `guards.py` resolves against the page, exactly the state the pair route's retraction exists to prevent. A **split** that would retract a veto the same operator wrote earlier is refused **409** until it is re-sent with `confirm_retract` — an assignment defaults every unnamed member into one unit, so a blank-slate save over an already-split group would otherwise reverse the earlier ruling for free. The one-unit assignment is therefore the split's UNDO, and the UI must keep it reachable (the row stays on screen once a split is stored).
- **E55** **The D6 session is measured on an UNBIASED, BLIND sample.** Two guarantees, both mechanical rather than remembered. (1) **Order.** Every review order the queues ship is a *working* order — weakest edge first puts the errors on top, score-descending puts the likeliest duplicates on top — so an error rate counted on one of them is an error rate about that slice, not about the engine. `sort=random` is a **seeded** total order (`md5(key || seed)`, the pair keyed `lo:hi`), stable across pages, reloads and days for one seed, and its cursor is that same hash: the sample cannot re-shuffle under the operator, and a second seed is simply a second sample with no schema of its own. The counter counts the **first 100** of that order, deliberately NOT narrowed by the filter bar — a sample that moved with the filters would mean something different on every page of one session. (2) **Blindness.** The residual queue hides every judge artefact — the chip, the verdict words, the key and contradicting evidence, the has-judgement hint — until the operator has recorded their own verdict on that pair, and reveals all of it the moment they have; the mode travels into the pair page on the URL, because a drill-down that showed the transcript on arrival is the hole in the blinding. It is ON by default there (that is where the gate's pairs are judged) and offered, off by default, on the groups queue. The ENGINE's own evidence — score, zone, families, contributions, "why it wasn't merged", the photos, the digests — stays visible throughout: it is not the thing being validated. Without both halves the D6 number measures how persuasive the judge's chip is, which is the one thing it must not measure.
- **E56** **The residual cohort is also served as CANDIDATE GROUPS, and every residual pair sits inside EXACTLY ONE of them.** The pairs of one generation are not independent questions: measured on g4, **7,653 residual pairs (4,996 band + 2,216 merge-zone in propose-only cells) touch only 4,158 listings**, 2,909 of those listings sit in **two or more** pairs (max 31), and about **3,180 already belong to a merged g4 group** — so collapsing each listing into its group leaves **~3,660 distinct unit-to-unit relations**, and the pair queue was asking "advert X vs each member of group G" over and over. `autodedup/candidates.py` (pure, read-time, no migration) lifts the pairs to **unit** level — an existing cluster of that generation, **all of its members, locked together**, or a lone unclustered listing — keeps the best pair score per unit edge, and packs the units by **greedy union in descending score under a hard cap of 8 adverts** per card. An edge that would burst the cap is **deferred to the next round**, where the units start alone again, and forms a smaller card of its own: **nothing is dropped**, every residual pair lands in exactly one card, and a test proves it. Two **locked** units that alone exceed the cap are forced onto one card instead — a unit is never split here, so the cap yields to the lock; it is a display budget, not a rule about the data. A unit MAY appear on more than one card (that is the price of the cap); a PAIR may not. The key is the smallest listing id plus a short digest of the sorted member ids, so it moves when the membership does and a stale link opens nothing rather than a repacked group. The build is memoised per (generation, store fingerprint: pair count, newest `decided_at`, lock count), so paging rebuilds nothing and a new score run or a re-clustering rebuilds everything. **The card never writes inside a lock**: the pairs of one already-merged group are the Groups page's ruling, and `POST /autodedup/verdict/candidate-split` neither confirms them nor retracts the must-not-links a split there wrote — an assignment that separates two adverts of one lock is a **400** naming the group and the page that owns it. There is no cluster row, so **no cluster verdict and no reason chips** (§9 keeps chips off a pairwise fan-out; the note still travels, and a `reasons` list is refused rather than silently dropped).
- **E57** **A refused BRIDGE is re-offered once, and applied only when the MERGED member set holds.** E37 refuses every edge whose two sides are already multi-member clusters, because one wrong bridge corrupts two properties at once. Measured on the operator's g4 rulings that refusal is where the engine's recall goes: of the 100 explicit merge-zone labels the clustering pass refused, **74 are bridges and every one of them is operator-`same`** — five portals carrying one advert split into two clusters, 51 of the 74 being the same-portal re-posts the operator has ruled MUST merge. E57 narrows E37 instead of lifting it: after the main pass, each recorded bridge is offered once more in the same certificate-first order, and applied **only** if it is a certificate or scores at least `bridge_min_score` (0.999) **and** the merged member set satisfies every invariant E34 states — must-not-link, size, category, area spread, disposition, floor spread — exactly as an ordinary union does. Nothing is fitted to the labels: the gate is the union rule the engine already had, applied to a set it previously declined to test. A bridge it does not apply keeps its row and names the invariant that refused it, so no refusal becomes invisible, and the pass is OFF unless a settings row asks for it (`bridge_apply`), so E37 stands wherever it is not enabled.
- **E59** **A benchmark label may come from a FACT THE ADVERT PRINTS — never from a model, and never from a column a portal parsed.** W6 left one binding constraint: 18 band negatives over 16 listings, 14 of them from one operator card, so no false-merge rate below ~20 % is demonstrable (D17). `autodedup/structural_truth.py` adds the missing axis without a judge and without the operator. **POSITIVE** — a Czech agency stamps its order number into the body ("evidenční číslo zakázky N115423", "Ev. číslo: 657349", "[ID 84553]") and the SAME string travels onto every portal the order is syndicated to and onto the re-post; a shared rare code is therefore ONE order (`pos_ref_cross`, `pos_ref_relist`), and one address block naming the same unit designator at the same area is one flat (`pos_unit_in_project`). **NEGATIVE** — co-liveness alone proves nothing, because a broker's two adverts of one flat on one portal are duplicates by the standing ruling, so every negative demands an EXPLICIT conflicting unit fact printed on both sides: two different unit designators in one address block (`neg_unit_number_conflict`), or two co-live adverts whose bodies print floor areas no closer than 3 % (`neg_stated_area_conflict`). Three things are forbidden here and each was measured before it was refused: a **stored column** never decides (the `floor` column is `patro` on one broker and `NP` on the next — adjudicated `10831166 × 11652959`, identical 35 m² and 8 050 Kč, floor 4 vs 3, one unit; the `area_m2` column is a per-portal title parse — `469862 × 15290511`, both bodies print 72 m², columns 70 vs 72, one unit); a **differing order code is NOT a negative** (an agency re-numbers per portal — `395722 × 486034`, identical text, identical 7 974 910 Kč, N115815 vs N118731, one flat — the rule was built, produced nine band pairs, and was dropped when two of the nine read false); and an **identical asking price abstains**, because a development that prints 27,2 m² and 28,6 m² both at 5 499 000 Kč cannot say which is the typo. A pair a positive and a negative rule both claim is returned to nobody. Evidence travels as short scrubbed strings so every label is re-readable, and the rule's name travels with it.

- **E58** **A cluster-grain verdict binds a MEMBER SET, and a generation never rewrites another generation's rows.** A cluster key is the smallest listing id ever admitted to the group (E36), so two passes over one cohort produce the SAME keys — and `autodedup.clusters` was keyed on `cluster_key` alone, with `generation` a plain column. Promoting g5 therefore re-stamped 836 of g4's 870 clusters through the score lane's own `on conflict (cluster_key) do update`: **g4 fell to 34 clusters, `mode=labels generation=g4` stopped being reproducible from the store, and the operator's 224 cluster-grain `same` verdicts silently re-attached to g5 groups** — 203 with an identical member set, **11 that GREW by an E57 bridge** (keys 38324, 46536, 69651, 86335, 200790, 312776, 337184, 414220, 425618, 530172, 13850718) and **10 absorbed into another key**. Nothing was corrupt; the store simply never recorded what a ruling was about. Two halves, both mechanical (migration 538). (i) **The generation is part of the key**: `clusters` is `(generation, cluster_key)`, `cluster_members` `(generation, cluster_key, listing_id)`, `pairs` `(generation, listing_lo, listing_hi)`; the score lane writes and deletes strictly inside its own generation — the sweep's old "delete by incoming key, whichever generation holds it" disjunct is gone, because it WAS the cross-generation reach — and every read joins on the generation as well. Pruning an old pass is a separate, opt-in act (`keep_generations=<n>`, off by default, ≈40 MB a generation on the trial cohort). (ii) **The ruling carries its set**: `verdicts.member_ids` is the sorted listing ids the SERVER resolved for that `(generation, cluster_key)` at write time — never a client's, whose idea of the membership is as old as its last fetch — and `verdicts.generation` names the pass. **The cluster-grain uniqueness carries the generation too** (`autodedup_verdicts_cluster_gen_uidx`, unique on `(kind, cluster_key, coalesce(generation,''), decided_by)`): the old index allowed ONE cluster ruling per key per operator, so ruling a g5 group UPDATEd the row holding the g4 ruling — the same "one key, last pass wins" defect, on the one table with no history behind it. A re-ruling can only ever replace a ruling of the SAME pass. A verdict **APPLIES** to a group only when `member_ids` equals that group's current member set; otherwise the group reads as **unreviewed** and carries a stale-verdict hint (the earlier ruling, its generation, which adverts arrived and which left). **The ruling that applies wins, and only when none does is the latest carried as the hint** — one ordering (`applies` first, `decided_at` second), shared by the Groups queue and the progress strip, so a key holding a ruling per pass can never read as unreviewed on one surface and done on the other, the SPA shows it as an ochre notice with the new adverts marked on their own cards, the verdict filter gains **"změněno od verdiktu"**, and the progress strips count only rulings that apply. The agreement read and the labels lane derive their implied pairs from `member_ids`, never from the current clustering. **Pair-grain verdicts and `must_not_link` stay generation-free by design** (a pair ruling is about two listings and says as much about g4 as about g5), and the table's own CHECK enforces it. A LEGACY row that carries no set — taken before 538 — falls back to the members of its own generation, and, where it names no generation either, only where the key belongs to exactly ONE pass: a bare key spans passes now, and unioning their members would invent operator labels nobody gave. Inventing a set out of today's clustering is the defect, not the fix.
- **E60** **A shared agency ORDER CODE is a certificate: K-R.** Two adverts that print the same rare agency order number (`evidenční číslo zakázky N115423`, `Ev. číslo: 657349`, bazos's `[ID 84553]`) are two views of ONE order, and one order is one unit — the string travels onto every portal the order is syndicated to and onto the re-post when the advert expires. It is the only evidence in this program that is a broker's **statement of identity** rather than an inference from resemblance, so it is read **before** K-A/K-B/K-C and it satisfies E45 on its own. Two purity rails, both from W7's census: a code carried by more than `MAX_CODE_POPULATION` = 8 listings is a per-broker sequence number (largest certifying population observed: 6, so the cap has never bound), and a code whose carriers disagree about the unit — two dispositions, or stored areas more than 5 % apart — is a PROJECT code and certifies nothing. E2/E3 and must-not-link are unchanged and still run first and last. A **DIFFERING** code is evidence of nothing in either direction (W6b built the conflict rule, W7 refuted it on 395722 × 486034 / × 496635: one flat, N115815 on ceskereality and N118731 on sreality, identical text, identical 7 974 910 Kč), so the `ref_code_shared` feature is PRESENT only when a code is shared and no refit can learn a penalty from a disagreement that means nothing. Measured (M51): 189 certificates, band −2.87 %, operator merge recall 79.01 % → 80.13 %, **0 operator and 0 gold false merges**, and a fact census of all 189 pairs finds 0 disposition conflicts, 0 stated-area gaps > 5 %, 0 conflicting unit designators.
- **E61** **Two different unit designators inside one address block are a VETO.** A developer's adverts in one building are near-identical by construction — one address, one shoot, one template, often one price — so no resemblance separates them; the building's own naming does. When both bodies name **exactly one** unit (`byt (č.3)`, `označením B36`, `jednotka č. 12`) and the names differ at one `address_block_key`, the pair is vetoed at the rule floor, before any certificate and any score, and the two strings travel on `Decision.evidence`. Three abstentions keep it a fact: a body naming several units is a price list and says nothing, a side naming none says nothing, and two buildings number their flats independently so the block must match. The veto refuses a **union**, not only an edge — vetoed pairs join the clustering's must-not-link set. Measured (M52): 45 pairs, 27 of them out of the band, 0 operator-`same` and 0 gold-`same` demoted. **Its evidence is 3 facts in ONE development** (ruian:21778370, units 1/3/5) for the band shrink; the other 18 pairs were already rejected and carry 9 more facts in 7 blocks. Never quote n=45 as a rate, and never score this veto against `neg_unit_number_conflict` — same rule, same parser.
- **E62** **An advert's live window ends at its last SIGHTING, never at the delisting-detection stamp** (`dataset.live_end_stamp`). `inactive_at` is when rule #3 NOTICED the advert was gone — after a near-complete index walk plus a cadence-scaled staleness window — and the lag reaches 70.7 days in the g5 cohort (median 0.57 d, p90 17.6 d, over 3,120 inactive listings). The benchmark always reads the sighting clock, because a LABEL may not be wrong to spare a guard. The ENGINE reads it only under `live_window_from_sighting`, which ships **OFF**: the truer clock is also the looser one for every co-live test it feeds (K-B's disjointness, E46, E47), and turning it on adds 503 merges of which 12 contested ones are a single ceskereality developer's 21 identically-priced 27 m² adverts (M53). Flipping it needs the hazard context AND a refit — `overlap_days` and `gap_days` move on 7,026 stored pairs.
- **E63** **A band pair carrying a near-identical BODY, an identical PRICE and a top-of-range score merges; hazard CONTEXT does not widen a cut.** W8a's hypothesis was that a false merge lives in a context — a dense development — and that outside it the engine could merge at a lower bar. Verification refuted the strong form on W8a's own labels and E63 is what survived. **What was refuted.** The proposed rule's context arm carried BOTH of its labelled errors (one on the sealed split, n=22, 95 % upper 21.8 %) while its context-free arm carried none and moved MORE pairs (347 against 337); the only clause encoding context — the one-shape-stack test — was **inert**, since dropping it moved 60 further pairs with the identical negatives and every one of the 23 labelled pairs it withheld was a duplicate; and the cohort's densest development (`ruian:21778370`, 51 listings) contributes 31 evidence-passing pairs of which every one is a correct merge, while the real leak is a 60-listing coworking product catalogue that a density test passes. **What ships.** One warrant: `containment_max ≥ 0.90` (the two bodies are near-identical) ∧ `price_last_ratio ≥ 0.995` (one current asking price) ∧ `score ≥ 0.9999` (the top of the isotonic range, well clear of the 0.9545 plateau where 41 negatives and 310 positives sit together) ∧ `area_rel_diff ≤ 0.01`. It re-reads only what **E11**, **E45** and **E48's propose-only cells** banded — never a veto, never an auto-reject, and **never E46 or E47**, the two developer guards the standing ruling is about. Three of the verification's five conditions are met although they bind only on a WIDER rule, because each is nearly free here: the area clause (C1, withholds 2 pairs and 0 labelled); an image floor an interior arm may never go below, enforced by `Settings.validate` rather than remembered (C2; the arm itself is off); and the **fungible-catalogue veto** (C3) — a stated FROM price, or a warrant whose every SHARED image is carried by ≥ 10 listings, or an address block of ≥ 20 same-category listings. Each limb is its own settings row because each cost was measured: the first two withhold 3 of the 347 endorsed g5 pairs, the third withholds 112 carrying **66 labelled duplicates**, so it is OFF and its cost is recorded rather than paid. Measured on the g5 cohort (M55): 273 promotions, band **5,180 → 4,907**, groups 872 → 939, **0 negatives in 62 labelled pairs, 0 negative blocks in 44, 0 in 19 sealed**, and it selects none of g5's 18 operator, 63 gold or 43 structural band negatives and none of the three pairs touching the disputed card 18705144. **OFF by default** and enabled by `autodedup/settings/w8.json`: W8a's 1,476-candidate search had already spent the sealed split (C5), so g6's sealed numbers CONFIRM a rule chosen elsewhere and cannot adjudicate between rules — a re-seal is owed before the next one.
- **E64** **A census is retrospective, so a merge taken under one is stamped with it and re-opened to the BAND when it is overtaken — never unmerged.** Every block- or population-census signal is monotone in the UNSAFE direction: 84.7 % of band cells grew after the moment their pair first became decidable (median 1.33×, p90 2.71×, max 50.5×), and 434 of 1,086 promoted sides had their first image gain carriers afterwards. A pair the fungible-catalogue veto would refuse today may have merged under a smaller census yesterday, and nothing revisits it. So each E63 promotion carries the census it was taken under (`context_cell_n`, `context_image_pop_min` on `Decision.evidence`, and the whole census on the stored row so a re-simulation replays the census the decision was taken under rather than today's), `hazard_context.rail_plan` names every stamped merge a limb's bar has since overtaken, and four invariants bound what may follow. **Re-opening is a demotion to the band**, never a direct `listings.property_id` write — an actual reversal is the operator's chokepoint (`toolkit/property_identity.unmerge_group`, replaying `property_merge_events`). **Certificates are exempt**: a shared order code, a disjoint-window re-post, an exact phash set and an identical price path are per-pair FACTS that no later census can decay. **Operator state is a stop, not a silent re-open** — a curated property (rule #18) or a pipeline card (rule #22) is surfaced instead. And the re-opening is **capped per address block**, so a block that doubles overnight re-opens a few merges rather than all of them, with the deferred ones counted by name. The rail is owed only by a settings row that switches a census limb on; with every limb off, no census enters the warrant and no rail is owed — which is the configuration the W8 verification preferred.
- **E65** **A certificate may not rest on photographs it never compared: K-B carries an image floor.** Every clause of K-B — one portal, one broker, a body containment ≥ 0.90, a stated area within 1 %, windows that never touched — is satisfied **by construction** when a broker re-posts one template across several units of one project, so the certificate asserts a unit while observing none. It survived only because the delisting-DETECTION clock inflated an inactive advert's window by up to 70.7 days and manufactured the overlap K-B's disjointness clause reads as "not a re-post"; the honest clock (**E62**) removes that accident and K-B goes 1,110 → 1,600 on the g6 cohort, 490 of the new ones in the image-less cell. The guard written for exactly this shape cannot reach it: **E46 requires `overlap_days > 0` and K-B requires 0, so the two are disjoint by construction** — that, not a vacuous comparison, is the hole. Measured (**M58**): of 1,600 K-B merges under the honest clock **1,348 carry zero comparable frames**, and **not one** of them is an advert without photographs — 1,318 have `catalog_ratio_max` 1.0, i.e. E9 subtracted the whole gallery. So the floor is two settings rows, read on **both sides** (`n_images_min`, E9-subtracted) and on the **matched set** (`phash_loose_matches` — K-C's tight bar asks "the same shoot", this asks only whether anything was compared and agreed); an **ABSENT** match count fails it, because nothing to compare is not evidence of agreement. Both default to 0 and `Settings.validate` refuses `live_window_from_sighting` without them, so the honest clock cannot ship without its price. **And the price is high, for a reason worth stating (M59): E9 cannot tell a re-post chain from a catalogue.** A frame is "stock" when ≥ `catalog_df` (8) listings carry it, so a broker who re-posts one advert nine times makes their own gallery stock — which is exactly the shape K-B exists to certify. 1,317 of the 1,348 image-less pairs share a raw frame **exactly**; 961 of those are carried by ONE broker on ONE portal (133 reliable labels, **133 positive, 0 negative**), while both sealed gold negatives sit in the multi-carrier cell. The floor as specified therefore withholds the honest chain along with the hazard, at **−8.5 points of reliable merge recall on dev**; the separator the evidence actually points at is **who else carries the shared frames**, and it is a W10 hypothesis, not a shipped rule — the dev split holds **zero** reliable labels in the multi-carrier cell, so it cannot be chosen here.
- **E68** **A published cut needs a VARIANCE TERM and DAYLIGHT: a zero-negative count is neither.** D3's gate is a Wilson lower bound because a count of clean merges says nothing about how many there were; W9 replaced it — no cell in the cohort can reach the ~381 consecutive correct merges 0.99 needs, and the pooled tier lets the cheap vision judge close a cell against D19 — with "smallest cut merging ≥ 30 dev pairs and carrying zero operator/gold negatives" over **337** (`model|cross`) and **17** (`model|same`) dev labels. That rule does not merely risk reading a cut off one pair, it **guarantees** it: the answer is by construction the first rung above the top labelled must-not-link. Measured (**M65**): `model|same` shipped **1.39e-05** above an operator `same_project_different_unit` (76 097 × 265 936 — two 60 m² 2+kk at 26 000 and 27 000 Kč under one broker in one project) with **two** stored pairs between them, and `model|cross` **9.96e-05** above a gold `same_building_different_unit` with **one**. The program had already refused this shape twice by hand — D16 (iv) at 3e-5, D18/M43 (ii) at 7e-5 — so it is a rule now: `stratum_thresholds` keeps the Wilson bound as the variance term and adds `CUT_MARGIN` (1e-3, ten rungs of the published grid) of daylight above the nearest labelled negative the cut leaves below it. The guard lifts a cut to the first rung with daylight rather than withholding it, binds only where a cut actually decides pairs by score (a certificate cell merging at 1.0 decides nothing by score), and when no admissible cut exists the cell is propose-only for a THIRD reason — `margin`, which is neither a modelling failure nor a labelling budget.
- **E69** **A seal that a search has read cannot DECIDE, and a seal that cannot CONTAIN a challenger cannot adjudicate it.** Two halves of one discipline. (i) **Spent.** W8a ran a 1,476-candidate search on `37c8771f…`, so every g6 sealed number CONFIRMS a rule chosen elsewhere; `seals.SPENT_SEALS` records it with what spent it, the file stays — an incumbent must remain re-measurable on the split it was fitted on — and what is gone is the power to choose. A committed map also carries the SEED that partitions it (`{"seed": n, "groups": {…}}`), because `split_of` hashes `<seed>:<group>` and one map under two seeds was two holdouts under one name. (ii) **Containment.** A map is cut from the merge components that existed when it was sealed, so a challenger merging pairs the incumbent banded produces components the map never saw; each listing it is missing falls back to its own id and hashes into a split of its own. Measured on W9 (**M67**): the fresh seal unions g6's and `honest_nofix`'s edges and contains both (0 spanning components), while the g7 candidate spans **75 components over 231 listings**, off **151** listings the map does not hold — so the holdout can measure g7's PAIRS but not a cluster-grain claim about it. `evaluate.components_spanning_split` reports it and `harness evaluate` warns; the fix is a seal cut from BLOCKING (every pair the engine could ever score, whatever it decides), or one unioned with the challenger's own edges before the challenger is measured.
- **E70** **A generation runs under a FROZEN CALIBRATION.** Six inputs of a decision are read off the cohort and not off the pair: K3's price deciles, the exploded-key set, the block and corpus document frequencies behind `tfidf` / `rare_token_overlap`, the in-block attribute frequencies behind `attr_rarity`, the exact-pin population, E60's certifying-code set — and, because `public.images.phash` carries no index and D8 forbids adding one, the corpus-wide pHash population E9's catalogue subtraction reads. Left live, each one makes a pair's score a function of WHEN it was scored: the same two adverts would land in different zones on Tuesday and on Friday, a re-run would silently re-decide yesterday's pairs, and `anchor_bands` — hence the K4 probe itself — would move under the engine. So they are computed once from a seed cohort, stored whole (`autodedup.rt_calibration`, migration 539), content-hashed, and stamped on every pair the generation decides. Recalibrating is not an update: it is a **new generation**, which is also the only moment E64's census can have grown, so the rail is owed exactly there. Frozen does not mean stale-forever — it means a decision can always be read back under the numbers that took it.
- **E71** **An arrival re-probes its whole probe-key NEIGHBOURHOOD, and a pair is kept while EITHER side retrieves the other.** `BlockIndex.candidates` caps fan-out at `max_candidates_per_listing` (297 of g6's 5,687 listings sit at the cap, M7), and a cap makes retrieval a function of what was present when it ran — the one place where "as listings arrive" could diverge from "over a cohort". The fix is not a bigger cap. When a listing's index keys change, every listing that PROBES one of those keys has its retrieval recomputed as well; because the ±1 relation on area band and price decile is symmetric, that neighbourhood is exactly the set `probe_keys` reaches, so it is one lookup and not a second widening. Each side's own retrieval is then recorded as a direction bit (`pairs.from_lo` / `from_hi`), and a pair survives while either bit stands — which is precisely the union the cohort pass takes over every listing's capped retrieval. Measured: the final pair set matches the cohort pass's **48,908 of 48,908** with 0 pairs on either side alone, and 90 pairs are created and later dropped along the way (94 under a shuffled arrival order), which is the cap churn this rule exists to correct rather than to hide.
- **E72** **Clustering is recomputed per CONNECTED COMPONENT of the merge-edge graph, never per edge.** Union-find over an edge stream is order-dependent; `cluster_pairs` over a component's whole edge set is not, because every rule it applies reads only the two clusters being joined — the certificate-first order (E33), `cluster_invariants_ok` on the merged member set (E34), E37's bridge refusal and E57's second offer — and those members never leave the component. So a pass re-clusters only the components whose merge edges or E61 vetoes moved, from their whole edge set, and the result is the cohort pass's result. A component larger than `max_component` is NOT clustered from a partial member set: it is named on the pass summary and left to the cohort lane, because a partial component is the one input that would make this differ. Measured on g6: **992 components over 4,690 merge edges, p50 2, p95 6, max 33** against a cap of 400, so the cap has never bound. Cluster identity stays E36's smallest admitted id, which inside one pass is the min of the members either path computes.
- **E73** **A watermark feed is settle-lagged, paged on its FULL key, and proved by an anti-join — and a delisting has an UNDO that needs a feed of its own.** Neither thing that looks like a watermark on `public` is monotone. `listings.id` and `listing_snapshots.id` are assigned at INSERT and not at COMMIT, so two overlapping portal-drain transactions commit out of id order and a bare `id > cursor` steps over the slower one for ever; `inactive_at` is `now()` — the TRANSACTION timestamp — so one `mark_inactive` batch shares one stamp, and a `limit` that cut a tie group skipped its tail permanently (live, 2026-09-20: 434,274 inactive rows over 141,087 stamps, largest group **5,534**, and **189 groups larger than one pass's share — 33,108 listings whose flips would never have been claimed**). So every feed ignores rows younger than a settle lag (300 s, against portal transactions that commit in seconds), the flip feed pages on `(inactive_at, id)` and never on the stamp alone, and the new-row feed carries an anti-join sweep of the last 5,000 ids against this generation's fingerprint rows — which PROVES a row was not missed instead of assuming it. The undo is `touch_listings`: it un-delists in place (`is_active = true, inactive_at = null`, same id) and appends no snapshot (rule #2), so a revival is invisible to all three forward cursors and needs the fourth — a bounded round-robin sweep of this generation's inactive fingerprints against the live flag (486,461 rows at 20,000 a pass ⇒ a ~4.1 h revival latency, which is the number to shorten if it ever matters). A flip moves K-B's disjointness and E46/E47's overlap, so none of this is bookkeeping.
- **E74** **A pass is bounded in STATEMENTS, not only in listings.** The engine decides a listing in under a second; the lane runs over the transaction pooler to Frankfurt, where a statement costs milliseconds of round trip whatever it asks for. Spelled one probe key at a time, one guard at a time and one posting insert at a time, a 166-listing claim issued **~17,700 statements** — 4.4 minutes at a realistic 15 ms RTT, against a 10-minute cadence. Every read and every write of a pass is therefore batched to the PASS's grain, not the listing's: one `unnest`-joined lookup for all of a working set's probe keys, one fingerprint read, one posting delete and one posting insert for the whole claim, `executemany` for the pair rows, and the census cells buffered and flushed once (measured: a whole 24-listing cohort decided in ONE pass costs **14 statements**, and a pass over an unchanged corpus costs **4**). A new statement inside a per-listing loop is the regression this rule names.
- **E75** **A pass is ALL-OR-NOTHING: it refuses a claim it cannot fit rather than truncating it.** `max_pairs` is a scheduling bound, and a truncation would silently turn it into a correctness one — W9's verification measured the truncating build on g6 at a cap of 500: it writes 13,127 of 48,908 pairs, re-clusters from that partial edge set, produces **38 member sets the cohort pass never produces**, and advances the watermark over the difference. So the pair budget is checked BEFORE the first write; over budget, the pass writes nothing, moves no cursor, and `run_pass_bounded` re-claims a smaller slice (the refusal is reported, and a refusal that survives the shrinking ladder STOPS rather than proceeding). The budget is SIZED from the measurement and not from a round number. Measured over g6 at three claim sizes: the densest pass wants **46,833** pairs at the shipped 500-listing claim, **45,154** at 200 and still **34,650** at 50 — so **shrinking the claim barely shrinks the pair set** (−26 % for a 10× smaller claim), because the dirty set is the BLOCK's neighbourhood either way and a block seen for the first time has to score its whole pair set however small the claim is (which is what E76's seed backfill pays once). The ladder therefore rescues a claim that spans many blocks and cannot rescue one dense block; the budget ships at **150,000**, 3.2× the measured maximum, and W9's 20,000 was a number nobody had measured against — it refused the replay outright. Holding the writes back is a mechanism and not a promise: steps 1–4 read and write through an overlay that only lands on `apply`, so a refused pass leaves no posting, no fingerprint row and no census bump behind — without that, the next, smaller attempt would find those listings "unchanged" and never score their pairs. Underneath, the whole pass is ONE transaction (`db.connect` is autocommit, so a crash between a cluster's DELETE and its INSERT would otherwise lose those clusters permanently: the re-claim finds matching digests, skips, and never rebuilds them).
- **E76** **A generation is SEEDED at the present, never cold-started from id 0.** The lane cannot run without a frozen calibration (E70) and it should not run behind one: with empty cursors its first pass claims `id > 0`, and at the shipped slice the changed feed needs **~83 days** to reach today's arrivals over 1.98 M snapshots. `--mode rt_seed` is therefore part of the lane and not a script: it cuts the calibration from the cohort artifact the batch pass scored, writes it whole to `rt_calibration`, stamps the four cursors at the corpus's current maxima, and (with `backfill=true`) writes the cohort's own fingerprints and postings so the generation starts from a populated store. A generation with no seed row refuses to run rather than guessing.
- **E77** **What the store reads back must be what the decision was taken as.** A cohort pass holds its decisions in memory; an incremental pass re-reads them, so every input of a LATER step has to survive the round trip. The certificate is a column and not a parse of `decision` (E63 re-promotes a certified pair under a `context_rule:` reason, so the string is lossy) because E33 orders a component's edges certificate-first and E57 gates its second offer on it; the evidence families are a BITMASK and not a count, because `cluster_rows` counts them and the UI filters `families & 32`; both fingerprint digests are columns, because they are what a re-score depends on; and the census cell a listing is counted in is stored, because a listing that MOVES has to unbump the cell it LEFT and `n_listings` is the exact counter `fungible_catalogue` and E64's rail read. Each of those four was a defect that the in-memory twin could not see and that a replay through the SQL store does: the store is proved by re-running the cohort through it, never by reading it. **The same rule binds the other end — a listing's FACTS are whatever the export lane reads, through the export's own statements and builders.** W9 hand-wrote a thinner set: CI's schema gate caught the loud half (`loc.lat` does not exist; `listing_location` keeps a `geom`), and behind it sat the silent half, which no replay over an exported artifact can see — no `attrs`, so every attribute feature would have been ABSENT in production while the proof had them; no price history, so E19's price events; no `broker_key`, so the whole BRK family; and `granularity` read as an enum rather than as text. The lane now reads `COHORT_LISTINGS_SQL` / `COHORT_LOCATION_SQL` / `COHORT_PRICE_HISTORY_SQL` / `COHORT_IMAGES_SQL` / `COHORT_CLIP_SQL` / `COHORT_CLIP_TAGS_SQL` and assembles them with `build_listing_record` and `build_image_record`; the ONE deliberate difference is the pHash population, which E70 freezes into `autodedup.phash_pop` instead of re-scanning `public.images`.
- **E79** **The real-time lane holds a SCOPE, and the scope is a required setting rather than a default.** W9's lane claimed the arrivals of the whole corpus: measured live, **178,190 new listings in 30 days against a corpus of 872,604**, each one a fingerprint row, **17.596 index keys** and a pair fan-out, into a store the operator pays for by the megabyte (schema `autodedup` is **148 MB** today). At the per-row costs this schema itself shows — 210 B for a `fp_key` row, 2,869 B for a pair row — that is **~3.4 GB every 30 days**, plus ~9.6 GB to seed. So `rt_scope` (a list of `{grain, code}` over `obec` / `cast_obce`) is REQUIRED: it defaults to the three trial blocks — Jablonec 563510, Turnov 577626, Praha-Vysočany 490245, derived from `cohort.BLOCKS` rather than retyped, the assembled negative control excluded because it is a rule with no arrival feed — an **empty or missing scope is a hard error**, and a whole-corpus run needs the explicit `rt_scope = all` AND a `rt_max_schema_mb` that could hold what `all` costs (17,000 MB). Under the scope the same three feeds carry **1,097 new + 1,600 content changes + 1,099 flips in 30 days = 0.88 arrivals a pass**, and the store grows **0.70 MB a day, 21 MB a month** — 162× less than unscoped. Three mechanisms make that real rather than aspirational. (a) **The predicate is index-served without an index of its own**: `listing_location` has a btree on `(obec_kod, granularity)` and NONE on `cast_obce_kod`, and D8 forbids DDL on a shared table, so every feed reads a bounded WINDOW off its own cursor index and resolves the scope for that window's ids through `listing_location_pkey` (`EXPLAIN` on the live statements: Index Scan on `listings_pkey` / `listing_snapshots_pkey` / `listings_inactive_at_idx`, then a Nested Loop of one `listing_location_pkey` probe per window row) — the scope is never the driving side, so the plan cannot degrade as the list grows. The **cursor advances over the WINDOW, not over the survivors**, because a feed crossing a corpus that is 99.4% out of scope at arrival speed would never arrive; when more rows survive than the claim's share the list is cut and the cursor stops at the last row taken, so no cursor ever passes a row the pass did not decide. (b) **A listing that LEAVES the scope is retired, not left half-indexed** — a geocode correction moves a listing without touching any of the four cursors, so a FIFTH feed round-robins this generation's own fingerprint rows against the scope and hands back the ones that left; retirement drops the postings, the fingerprint row and the pairs, and re-clusters the components those edges held together (half-indexed is worse than unindexed: every neighbour would go on retrieving through postings nothing maintains). (c) **A pass reads what the schema costs before it writes anything** and REFUSES over `rt_max_schema_mb` (default 400 MB) — loudly, non-zero, cursor unmoved — because the failure this guards is not a crash but a silent lane nobody watched for a week; each summary reports schema MB, rt rows by table and the growth since the previous pass. And the store keeps what the BATCH lane keeps: `score_lane.storable` — the whole merge and band zones plus the reject tail at or above `store_floor` — which on the trial cohort is **13,043 of 44,724 decisions (29%)**, so the store grows with duplicates and not with comparisons. W9d adds the three rails the scope OWED, each one reproduced as a defect before it was closed (§11a). (d) **An empty scope is unconstructible, and the drift sweep refuses on its own feet anyway** — a separator-only `rt_scope` parsed to a NULL scope whose sweep reported the WHOLE store as departed, so `Scope` now refuses to exist without a block, and independently of that the sweep retires nothing under an empty scope and STOPS the lane — non-zero, cursor unmoved — when it would retire more than `rt_max_retire_fraction` (default **0.05**) of the generation in one pass window. (e) **The SEEDED scope is the generation's scope**: `rt_seed` persists it, a pass runs it, and a dispatch argument that differs is refused unless `rt_rescope=true` moves the generation (persisting the new scope and restarting the entrant sweep); a seeded generation with no `rt_scope` row is a hard error rather than a silent default. (f) **A scope is watched in BOTH directions**: `listing_location` is written after the listing is, so a SIXTH feed round-robins the scope's own listing ids one block a pass — the `(obec_kod, granularity)` index for a town, a quarter's parent obec filtered on `cast_obce_kod` for a quarter (there is no index on that column and D8 forbids one) — and claims what has no fingerprint row; the straggler sweep beside it counts ROWS (`order by id desc limit N`) and no longer an id range.
- **E78** **An operator refusal is reconciled on EVERY pass, including an idle one.** Re-clustering is seeded by pairs whose zone moved, and a `must_not_link` row an operator adds after the merge it refuses moves no pair — so without a second seed it would never be honoured (reproduced: `components_reclustered 0, pairs_scored 0`, the two listings still in one cluster). Every pass therefore reads the operator's refusals and seeds the component of any pair that today's clusters contradict; the set is operator-curated and small, so it costs one membership read, and a pass with nothing to claim still runs it, because that is exactly when an operator has been working.
- **E80** **Seeding is the step BEFORE the switch, so the seed never consults the switch — and an unseeded lane is a green SKIP, not a failure every ten minutes.** W9 gated `rt_seed` on `AUTODEDUP_REALTIME_ENABLED` and the engine lane never passed that variable, so the recipe this document printed — `gh workflow run autodedup.yml -f mode=rt_seed …` — exited 0 having seeded nothing, and the operator who then flipped the variable got a `*/10` schedule hard-erroring on the missing calibration and the missing scope row for ever. So: `rt_seed` runs while the lane is dark (it is read-only against `public` and writes only schema `autodedup`); it takes `export_run=<the export lane's run id>` and downloads the same artifact the score and judge lanes do, because a runner holds no cohort file otherwise; it is IDEMPOTENT — a generation that already carries a frozen calibration is refused unless `reseed=true`, since a re-seed re-cuts the calibration every stored decision was taken under; and a pass over an unseeded generation returns `skipped: unseeded` with nothing written and nothing claimed. The enable sequence is five ordered steps and they are printed where the operator will look for them (`.github/workflows/autodedup_realtime.yml`, §11a): apply 539 → merge → seed → verify → flip the variable.
- **E81** **The entrant feed may not cost the production instance a scan per pass.** The sixth feed (E79 (f)) has no index-served path to a QUARTER — `listing_location` carries a btree on `(obec_kod, granularity)`, none on `cast_obce_kod`, `public.listings` carries no indexed obec or quarter column either (all three checked live against `pg_indexes`), and D8 forbids adding one — so its block walk is a bitmap heap scan of the parent obec filtered on the quarter: measured live with `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)`, **159,357 index rows → 1,439 rows, 29,265 heap blocks, 29,568 buffers = 231 MB, 6.4 s**, cold on every run because the working set does not stay in `shared_buffers`. W9d ran one block a pass, so each block was walked ~48 times a day: **12.1 GB a day of cold reads on the instance that serves Browse**, whether or not anything had entered, and `enter_slice` bounded none of it (the `limit` is applied after the bitmap). The walk is therefore a REFRESH of `autodedup.rt_scope_ids` — the scope's membership snapshot — on a cadence that is data (`rt_enter_interval_hours`: **6 h** a quarter, **1 h** a town), at most one block a pass, the longest-overdue block first, under a rolling-day cap that is data (`rt_enter_max_scans_per_day`, default 60) which the lane refuses to exceed. An ordinary pass then claims its entrants with an anti-join between two tables of schema `autodedup` and reads **zero blocks of `public`** for this feed: **12,434 MB a day → 1,597 MB a day (12.1 GB → 1.56 GB), 1,591,584 blocks → 204,432**. What a cadence costs is latency, and only on the TAIL: a listing whose location resolves shortly after it arrives is already the straggler sweep's (E73), so this feed exists for the long tail — an old listing re-geocoded into the scope — which is measured in days. And it is settle-lagged like every other feed, on `resolved_at` (the snapshot carries it, because the refresh reads that column anyway): the location's resolution IS this feed's arrival event.
- **E82** **Every rail of this lane is DATA — a row of `autodedup.settings`, keyed by generation where it belongs to one — and never a dispatch argument or a silent default.** Three defects, one rule. (a) `rt_scope` was ONE global row, so a second generation's seed silently rescoped the first and its drift sweep retired everything the new scope left out; the row is `rt_scope:<generation>` now, and the legacy global row is read exactly once, for generation `rt`, which is the only generation that can have written one. (b) `rt_max_retire_fraction` was a plain dispatch argument, so a single `args=rt_max_retire_fraction=1` disabled the store's last rail for that pass with nothing persisted and nothing said; the control numbers (`rt_max_schema_mb`, `rt_max_retire_fraction`, `rt_enter_max_scans_per_day`, `rt_enter_interval_hours`) are read from `autodedup.settings` and accepted on a dispatch only beside `rt_rescope=true` — the same word the operator already has to say to move a generation's scope. (c) A value that is not a number fell back to the default, so a rail ran at a number nobody had chosen; it is a hard error now. And the retirement rail itself is measured as data over TIME rather than over one slice: W9d compared the departed count of ONE drift slice with a fraction of the store, which is a rail only while the slice is itself that big — above ~400,000 rows, or under a `drift_slice=1` dispatch argument, a store-wide departure was reported a slice at a time and ground the store away unguarded (reproduced at 1/500 scale: 1,000 rows, slice 20, whole store departed, no refusal). Retirement is therefore counted over a ROLLING DAY — `autodedup.rt_retire_event`, written inside the pass's own transaction — against the store as it stood when that window opened, and over the fraction the lane STOPS: non-zero, transaction rolled back, cursor unmoved.
- **E83** **Carrier-aware catalogue subtraction: a frame is STOCK only when its carriers are several PARTIES.** E9 calls a frame stock when `images.phash` sits on at least `catalog_df` (8) listings corpus-wide, and that population test cannot tell a marketing catalogue from a broker re-posting ONE advert nine times — both put one frame on nine listings, and the second shape is precisely what K-B exists to certify. M59 priced the confusion at 228 withheld duplicates for 2 hazards; E83 is the separator D24 (vi) pointed at. A populous frame is exempted from subtraction when its carriers are **one broker, one source and one address block** (`catalog_min_broker_carriers` / `catalog_min_source_carriers` / `catalog_min_block_carriers`, each a settings row, combined by `catalog_carrier_combine` — `any` limb clearing its bar makes the frame stock, which is the conservative reading). Three rails, because every relaxation of E9 adds image evidence and therefore adds merges, so each unobservable case resolves the safe way. **(i) Coverage.** Carrier identity is observable only INSIDE the cohort while `pop` is corpus-wide, so the exemption needs `catalog_carrier_coverage_min` (default 1.0) of the population present: a frame the cohort sees 3 of 50 carriers of cannot be attributed to one party and stays subtracted. Of 1,269 populous frames in the g6 cohort the cohort fully contains 431 (M84). **(ii) Unknown identity is its own party** — a carrier with no broker key never collapses with another unknown. **(iii) Purity, E60's rail restated for a photograph:** a frame whose carriers disagree about the unit — two dispositions, or stated areas further apart than `catalog_carrier_area_tol` (0.01, K-B's own `CERT_B_AREA`) — holds the PROJECT's material whoever holds it, and stays subtracted. The rail is what separates the two families E9 confuses: the idnes 77-advert re-post chain's exempted frames carry an area spread of exactly 0.0 over 20-31 carriers, while the ceskereality developer's one exclusive frame spans 26 and 27 m2 (M85). OFF by default (`catalog_carrier_aware=false`), and **inert on the shipped generation**: on the detection clock it moves not one pair of g6's 4,690 merges, 4,907 band or 939 groups (M86). It earns its keep only under the honest clock, where it returns 931 of the 1,364 K-B certificates the E65 floor withholds while the floor still holds the developer at zero (M87). It is computed per pass from the cohort (`autodedup/stock.py`); a pass with no dataset falls back to plain E9, which is the conservative direction and what the incremental store does today — an incremental path that turns it on owes the index, or E70's replay equivalence breaks.
- **E84** **K-B's disjointness is read on the PAIR, and never below the resolution of the sightings behind it.** A live window is only as sharp as the sightings that drew it, and under the honest clock (E62) an advert seen exactly ONCE has `first_seen == last_seen`: its window is a point, so `max(starts) > min(ends)` — the whole of the disjointness clause — is satisfied by ANY two once-seen adverts, whatever else they are. The W10 gold labels caught it costing a merge: **412540 x 412544**, two *different Regus products* at one address (Na Strzi 1702/65: the 2,050 Kc coworking membership and the 3,790 Kc office membership), first seen **2.8 seconds apart in one index walk** and delisted together, certified K-B and merged at 0.9545 — gold `different_property`, unanimous, confidence 1.0, and the advert bodies support the verdict. g6 bands the same pair; the honest clock alone merges it, with or without E83. So K-B's gap carries a floor, `certificate_b_min_gap_days`, read on the PAIR (`decide.window_gap_days`) and never on one side: measured engine-wide over the candidate's 1,167 K-B merges, a **one-minute** pair gap withholds **exactly that pair and 0 labelled duplicates**, an hour withholds 3 (2 labelled duplicates), a day 147 (95), and "either side degenerate" 41 (7) — the side reading costs seven true re-posts for the same one hazard (M89). The row is **0 by default and REQUIRED above 0 under the honest clock** (`validate` refuses `live_window_from_sighting` without it, as it already refuses it without E65), because the two clocks measure different things: on the DETECTION clock the gap runs from `inactive_at`, where a sub-minute separation is a delisting *detected* and the successor listed in the same drain pass — g6's `18715382 x 18739520` is one broker's 29,000 Kc 2+kk re-listed **44 seconds** after it was found gone, a true re-post the same minute would withhold. Every honest-clock row in the repo therefore carries the rail (`w9_honest_arm`, `w9_g7_candidate`, `w10_honest_carrier`) and the shipped generation carries none, which is also why this is a rail and not a promotion.
- **E85** **K-B certifies a pair only when its FAMILY is consistent with ONE unit.** Every clause of K-B is pair-local — one portal, one broker, one template body, a stated area within 1 %, windows that never touched — and W11 read 77 K-B FAMILIES blind (a family = the connected component of the pairs K-B certified, **before** the guard runs, never of the pairs it merged or of what survives it, or the definition would move under its own verdict): **67 one unit re-posted, 9 several units of one project posted serially, 1 undecidable** (M93). The two shapes are indistinguishable pair by pair and E46 cannot reach them (it needs `overlap_days > 0`, K-B needs 0), which is the hole E65 closed with an image floor that costs 89 % of the one-unit chains' merges (M96). The separator is the CHAIN. A **cell** is a set of family members that could be one advert re-posted; a K-B pair keeps its certificate only when both sides sit in one cell, and a refused pair falls to K-C or to the model exactly as an uncertified pair does — the guard removes evidence, it never manufactures a contradiction (never a veto, never a reject, never K-R). Five clauses, each its own settings row because each was priced separately (M94): the **headline printed area** (the size the body states for this unit, read only inside `family_guard_area_window` of the stored one, differing by more than `family_guard_area_tol`), **concurrency** (two members live at once on the honest clock), **price** (two prices are one unit only when the later window's ask is not a RISE beyond `family_guard_price_rise_max` — a stepwise cut is a flat that is not selling, two prices alternating are two objects), **disposition** and **unit designator** (E61 restated at family grain; both inert on this cohort and recorded as such). A sixth, a DIFFERING agency order code, is a settings row that is **OFF and stays off**: it buys two adjudicated families and pays by contradicting **E60**, refusing E60's own worked example (M95). Cells are first-fit CLIQUES over members ordered by first sighting, not connected components: a 60 % price cut is compatible on its own, so components fuse two chains and then lose the whole family — the ceskereality 2+kk family loses all 107 of its K-B pairs as a component and only its 26 cross-chain ones as cells.
