Mode `cell` is the shipped shape of the rule, `family` (refuse every edge of an impure family) is measured and rejected at 4x the cost for the same coverage (M94). `Settings.validate` now lets the honest clock (E62) pay its price with E85 **or** E65, never with neither, and still never without E84. **OFF by default** (`family_guard_mode="off"`), and the incremental lane RAISES on it: a family is a property of the pair set a pass sees one slice of, and it only ever grows, so the rail owed is E64's — re-evaluation triggered by a family gaining a member rather than by a block census growing, demoting to the BAND and never unmerging, with K-B the one certificate E64's blanket certificate exemption must not cover. The batch side is built (`autodedup/family.py`); the incremental side is owed. **W11's verification then withdrew the structural case above** (M99): re-read blind against the adverts, the two ceskereality families that carry 64 of the guard's 77 refusals are each ONE unit — the 2+kk family's "two concurrent price chains" are one co-operative flat whose members all print `podíl 3 399 000` **and** `Anuita: 5 126 000`, and 3 399 000 + 5 126 000 = 8 525 000, while the 1+kk family's "28,6 vs 27,2" is a two-character edit in an otherwise byte-identical body that is corrected once at advert 3 of 21 and never reverts. The re-read finds **no** family that is unambiguously several units posted serially, and the sealed split then measured the guard changing NOTHING (M100). It stays DATA and OFF, its candidate row kept as a record at `settings/refuted/w11_candidate.json` (E87), and the real-time lane keeps g6's rules until it carries a family index.
- **E86** **A family guard's verdict must be an invariant of the FAMILY, not of the order its members arrived in.** E85 partitions a K-B family by first-fit over members ordered by first sighting, and first-fit is not confluent: re-partitioning the same families under 20 shuffled orders moves the partition on **4 of the 37 families of size >= 3**, one of them into three distinct partitions (M101). Which certificate survives is then a property of arrival order — the one thing a replay, a backfill or an incremental pass changes — so E70's replay equivalence cannot hold for it and E64's rail cannot repair it, because the pair another order would have refused is not the pair the rail re-opens. Three consequences, all shipped: `cell` and `family` are **batch-only** and their order is pinned to first sighting so a cohort pass and its replay agree (`tests/autodedup/test_family_guard.py` pins the pinning **and** the defect, by refusing a different edge when the same three adverts arrive in another order); a fourth mode **`pair`** refuses only an edge whose own two adverts are incompatible, which no third advert and no order can move, and it is the only mode an incremental rail may be designed on (M102) — the lane still raises on all three until that rail exists; and the run's own ordering is a rail too, since holding K-B rows back until the family can be read must change the verdict and nothing else: `harness.run_engine` merges the held rows back into key order and sorts the decision list before clustering, so `pairs.jsonl.gz` and the cluster edge order are byte-for-byte what a run without the guard writes (`test_engine_e2e.py::test_the_family_guard_does_not_reorder_the_run_artifacts`).
- **E87** **A gate may only accept a price a SEALED read has measured.** W11's build widened `Settings.validate` so the honest clock (E62) could pay its price with E85 *or* E65 — an argument made before the seal was opened. The seal then measured E85 **inert**: against the same arm without it, 0 sealed labelled duplicates gained, 0 lost, all 27 sealed demotions unlabelled, and the one sealed reliable false merge kept by design inside a cell (M100). A price that buys nothing is not a price, so the gate reverts: under the honest clock the E65 image floor is again the only payment `validate` accepts, and the honest clock's true price is an open question D30 (vii) owes, not a row this gate may assume. The refuted arm keeps its record at **`settings/refuted/w11_candidate.json`** — outside `settings/`, where every file must be constructible — and is built the way it was measured, by assignment, exactly as W10's no-floor arm was.
- **E88** **The honest clock ships everywhere EXCEPT where the hazard lives — and the two definitions that tried to say where were both measured too wide to ship.** W11 left one open path to the honest clock (D30 vii): a price `validate` could accept that is not the blanket E65 floor. E88 is that attempt, pre-registered and then refused by its own rule. **The mechanism.** A K-B certificate — the only merge path resting on the honest clock's disjoint windows — inside a new-development serial-poster FAMILY is **HELD**: the pair is re-decided with K-B withdrawn and falls to K-C, the model or E63, exactly as E85's refusal does — the hold removes evidence and never manufactures a contradiction, so it is never itself a veto or a reject, though the model that then reads the pair may of course score it under `t_lo` (20 of NARROW's 1,227 held pairs and 20 of WIDE's 1,523 land there, by the model's own verdict and no rule of this one). What is new is the PREDICATE, which reads the family's PROVENANCE (is this a development being sold serially?) rather than whether its members could be one unit. **The family** is the connected component of K-B-certified pairs restricted to edges inside one source, one broker and one address block — a connected component, so unlike E85's first-fit cells the partition is an INVARIANT of the member set and E86's order defect cannot reach it (`tests/autodedup/test_development.py` pins it under 20 shuffles). **The markers**, all readable at decision time: project vocabulary (`rezidenc`, `projekt`, `developer`, `novostavb`, `etap`, `cenik`, `kolaudac`, `dokonceni`) in at least half the members; co-operative financing vocabulary (`podil`, `anuit`, `druzstevn`) in at least half of a family of three or more; family size; block density (other same-category listings at the family's address); and members printing different unit-level facts, which is REPORTED and in neither definition, because M99 read that signal as a typo corrected once. **Two definitions, both written down and committed before the first arm ran**: NARROW = vocabulary AND size ≥ 3, WIDE = vocabulary OR size ≥ 4 OR block density ≥ 20 (E63's own C3 block bar, not a new number). **Measured (M104–M107): both do exactly what they were built to do and both cost more than the honest clock buys.** Each removes both contested gold negatives from the merge zone; each also withholds the honest chains beside them, because a broker's own re-post chain prints the same project vocabulary, reaches the same size and sits at the same dense address as a developer's serial catalogue. On dev + validation the honest clock gains **165** reliable labelled duplicates over g6; NARROW gives back **212** and WIDE **297**, so the pre-stated rule (the widest hold whose cost is under one third of the gain) names **no candidate**. The rule ships as DATA and OFF (`development_hold_mode="off"`), `Settings.validate` accepts it as an alternative price to the E65 floor (and refuses it without E84), and the real-time lane RAISES on it — with the one thing that is genuinely better than E85's position stated: the hold is **monotone** (a family only gains members, vocabulary only arrives, a census only fills up), so a family can only ever TURN development-like and the rail owed is nameable, E64's with a family trigger. **And one convention this wave had to fix to run at all**: its cohort, arms and construction reproduce W11's split map to the listing, so a fresh holdout could only be a fresh SEED — and under "a seal is named by its map" that seal could be committed only by overwriting a SPENT one. `seals.seal_id` now names a seed-bearing seal by map AND seed; the four seals cut before this rule keep their map-only names.
- **E89** **A price the seal measures NEGATIVE is not a price — the gate takes back E88's widening exactly as E87 took back E85's.** W12's build widened `Settings.validate` so the honest clock (E62) could pay with the E65 image floor *or* the E88 new-development hold, and said in the same breath that E87 still binds: a gate may only accept a price a SEALED read has measured. The W12 seal has now been opened once and measured it. On the test side the NARROW hold merges **145 of 238** reliable labelled duplicates against g6's **177** — McNemar gained 7, lost 39, exact two-sided p < 1e-5 — and removes **0** false merges, because there are none to remove: g6, the candidate and the honest arm all carry 0 reliable false merges at pair, block, family and cluster grain (M110). E85 was inert; the hold is NEGATIVE — it pays 32 true merges for no measured safety. So the gate reverts, the E65 image floor is again the only payment `validate` accepts, and the three rows that exercised the hold keep their records at **`settings/refuted/{w12_candidate,w12_narrow,w12_wide}.json`** — outside `settings/`, where every file must be constructible — built the way they were measured, by assignment, exactly as W10's no-floor arm and W11's guard arm were. **And the boundary the same read exposed (M111): a hold on K-B cannot be the honest clock's price at all**, because K-B is not the only channel the clock opens — 21 of the honest arm's 469 new merges over g6 arrive through the model (13 of them at exactly the `model|cross` threshold 0.9788), E63 (7) and K-R (1), since the clock moves the FEATURES and therefore the SCORE, and the hold keeps every one of them, four inside a real new development. A successor to E88 must act on the score, not only on the certificate; must carry a minimum family size on every limb (M109); and must reach the development that posts a unit only TWICE (M114), which no size bar ever will. The rule itself stays as DATA and OFF, and lifting it is a settings change — `development_hold_mode`, with `development.holds` live, tested and unreachable from the real-time lane.

<!-- W9g's three rules were written as E83/E83a/E84 and moved to E90/E90a/E91 (and M84-M88 to
     M150-M154, D28 to D33) when W10 landed on main first and took those numbers. Numbers are
     never re-used and never renumbered once merged; these had not merged yet. -->
- **E90** **A cohort statistic the lane READS but nothing WRITES is not frozen — it is absent, and absent is a value.** W9f's first live pass issued **1,024 K-B, 75 K-R and ZERO K-C** where the batch generation `g6` over the same cohort issued **1,771 K-C**, and 5,888 of the 7,878 pairs both generations hold moved in score. The replay-equivalence proof (44,724 of 44,724) could not see it, because replay feeds the incremental path from the export ARTIFACT while the live lane reads through `SqlFacts` — the same blind spot E77 names, at a different input. The input was the corpus-wide pHash population: E70 freezes it into `autodedup.phash_pop` and `SqlFacts` joins every image against that table, reading an absent hash as a population of **0** — but **no code in this repository ever writes that table**, and it holds **0 rows**. The export, by contrast, computes the population with a `count(DISTINCT listing_id)` over `public.images`, which cannot return less than 1 for a hash that exists. So in production every image carried `pop = 0`, `Image.pop_is_measured()` was false for every gallery, `build_fingerprint` published `catalog_ratio = None` for every listing, `catalog_ratio_max` was ABSENT on every pair — and K-C, which requires that ratio to be PRESENT, became structurally unreachable while K-B, which does not read it, went on firing. Three consequences follow from one missing table: a certificate that cannot be earned, E9's catalogue subtraction disarmed (nothing can reach `catalog_df = 8`, so stock photographs stay in the pHash, CLIP and tag galleries and crowd the `phash_sample` cap), and `ContextIndex.shared_image_population` reading 0 for every pair, which is the hazard limb E64's rail is built on. The rule is general: **a frozen statistic needs a writer, a row count and a refusal.** The seed that cuts a generation's calibration is the only place that can populate one, a lane whose frozen input is empty must refuse rather than read zeros as measurements, and `pop` must carry the export's distinction — `None` is UNKNOWN, `0` is a lie no real photograph can tell. `--mode rt_parity` is the instrument that finds this class: it builds a sample of listings twice, once from an export artifact and once through `SqlFacts`, diffs them field by field, and re-scores the same pairs from both fact sets under one settings/model/calibration — a defect that lives between the two readings has nowhere to hide from it.
- **E90a** **Which settings and which model a pass ran under is part of its result, and a dispatch default is not a choice.** The same live pass ran with empty `args`, so `load_settings(None)` gave `Settings()` and `load_model(None)` gave the uncalibrated `hand_initialised()` prior — `t_lo 0.30` against w8's `0.1823`, `t_hi 0.97` against `1.0`, no `t_hi_by_stratum`, no bridge, no context rule — while `g6` was scored under `w8` and `w6_gold`. The seed recorded it faithfully (`rt_calibration.model_version = 'hand_v1'`) and nothing read it back: `autodedup.pairs.model_version` is **NULL on all 15,923 rt rows**, so the store could not say which scorer had taken its decisions. A generation's settings and model belong to the generation, beside its calibration, and a pass must load them from there and stamp them on every row it writes — the frozen calibration pins the cohort statistics and pins neither.
- **E91** **A live lane's facts are built by the EXPORT's builders from the export's statements, and a PARITY SAMPLE is a gate on every pass — not an instrument you run when something looks wrong.** E77 made the lane read `COHORT_LISTINGS_SQL` and its five siblings through `build_listing_record` / `build_image_record`; W9f then shipped anyway, because the one input those builders do not read from `public` — the corpus-wide pHash population, frozen by E70 into `autodedup.phash_pop` — had no writer, held 0 rows, and was read as a population of **0** rather than as an unknown. One live pass, 36,940 pairs, ZERO K-C (E90). Three parts, and all three are the rule: **(a) the frozen statistic is carried by the SEED.** The number the live lane needs is the one the export already measured with `count(DISTINCT listing_id)` over `public.images`, so `rt_seed` copies it out of the artifact into `autodedup.phash_pop` — no scan of `public.images` at seed time and none at pass time, and the lane then joins against exactly the statistic the batch engine scored with. A seed whose artifact carries no measured population (`phash_pop_ok=false`) is REFUSED. **(b) A hash the frozen table does not carry is UNKNOWN (`pop = None`), never 0** — the export writes the same null when its own probe does not run, and `pop = 0` is a claim no real photograph can support (it appears on at least its own listing). There is no cheaper way to measure a new one: `public.images` carries no index on `phash` (checked live — `images_phash_idx` is on `sreality_id`), D8 forbids adding one, and no in-schema mirror of (hash → listing) exists, so the only measurement is the export's own sequential scan. **The staleness this implies is stated, not hidden**: the population is as of the export, it can only have grown, and a photograph that arrived after it is unmeasured — which costs that listing its `catalog_ratio` (conservative: K-C cannot fire for it) rather than asserting that its photo is unique. `population.images_unmeasured` on every pass summary is what says when the generation is due a fresh export and re-seed. **(c) Parity is a GATE.** The seed cuts a baseline of FOUR digests per sampled listing — `stored` (columns, location, attrs, price history, gallery identity and order), `sighting` (`last_seen_at`, `is_active`, `inactive_at`), `producer` (pHash, CLIP vector, CLIP tags) and `population` (the frozen count per hash) — into `autodedup.settings` beside the calibration, and every pass re-checks a fresh slice of it BEFORE it opens its transaction. Tolerance **zero**: a `stored` or `population` digest that differs on a listing whose content has not changed since the export stops the pass — loud, non-zero, nothing written, no cursor moved. Producer movement is REPORTED and not refused, because the pHash and CLIP jobs re-run over the corpus on their own cadence and a frozen calibration cannot ask them not to — and so is the SIGHTING clock (`last_seen_at`, `is_active`, `inactive_at`), which rules #4 and #3 move with no snapshot behind either. `--mode rt_parity` stays what it is: the field-by-field report you run when the gate refuses.
- **E92** **A decision records the EVIDENCE it rested on, and a feed brings the listing back when that evidence arrives.** W9g proved the live lane reads the export's facts about a listing the export CARRIED; it said nothing about an arrival, and an arrival is decided before its photographs are evidence. Measured: images land **~1 minute** after `first_seen_at`, `images.phash` is written by an hourly job at **:20** and the CLIP vector and tags by another at **:40** (**p50 2.47 h, p90 4.51 h** to a first tag), while the lane claims at `first_seen_at + 5..15 min`. So **~80% of arrivals were decided with every `phash` NULL**, **78% never carry a second snapshot**, and not one of W9's six feeds is keyed on a hash or a vector ARRIVING — a phash appends no snapshot, flips no flag and moves no location. The listing was decided once, blind, and never again: its stored index keys never gained a pHash posting either, so **no other listing's photo probe could retrieve it** for the life of the generation (pass 2 claimed 0, and the keys still carried none). Stripping photo evidence from the artifact's own pairs moves 15 of 400 scores past 0.01, 8 zones (4 merge→band, 4 reject→band) and **every K-C**. The rule has two halves and this is the first: **`rt_fp` records what the decision rested on** — `ev_images`, `ev_phash`, `ev_clip`, `ev_tags` and `first_decided_at` (migration 540) — and a **seventh feed** round-robins this generation's rows whose evidence is incomplete or whose first decision is inside `rt_evidence_horizon_hours` (data, **48 h**), probes `public.images` by listing id and re-claims every listing whose photographs no longer match. The probe is the only read of `public.images` outside a fact fetch and it is bounded like every other cost on this lane: `images_listing_id_idx`, **12.4 buffers a listing** (2,484 buffers / 3,085 images / 200 listings, 17 ms warm), and it reads NO other table because `images.clip_tagged_at` is the CLIP job's own stamp — measured identical to `image_clip_embeddings` and `image_clip_tags` on **73,208 of 73,208** trial-scope images, at a fourteenth of the buffers the two joins cost (M115, M116). A re-claim rewrites the postings (so the pHash band exists) and re-scores the pairs; `first_decided_at` is coalesced, never restamped, so a re-decision cannot restart the horizon.
- **E93** **Do not act on evidence you do not have yet: a MERGE that rests on photographs WAITS for them; a reject does not.** The half E92 owes, and the reason the sweep is not enough on its own: a listing claimed blind can be merged blind — 19 of the trial cohort's merges are (M118) — and E92 would then re-open a group rather than never form it. So while either side's gallery **exists and carries no hash at all** and that side is inside the horizon, a merge that does not rest on a certificate needing no photograph — **K-B** (one portal, one broker, one body, live windows that never touched) or **K-R** (a shared agency order code), both text-and-clock facts — is **HELD in the band** with reason `evidence_pending`, carrying what it was holding (`held_zone`, `held_reason`, `held_certificate`) and certifying nothing. Rejects are never held: a pair refused on text, price or geometry is refused on evidence the lane HAS, and holding it would be the anti-conservative direction. The hold is released when the evidence lands **or** when the horizon passes — and the second case moves no fact at all, so the sweep's second arm asks for the held pairs BY REASON (`decision = 'evidence_pending'`) rather than waiting for a digest that will never move. **What it costs, honestly**: a text-or-certificate decision still completes within the pass that claims the listing; a photo-dependent merge waits for the hourly producers, so its arrival-to-merge time is that distribution — p50 2.47 h, p90 4.51 h, bounded at 48 h — rather than the engine's sub-second. The production change that would remove the wait is **hashing on download in the image worker** (`scraper/`), which is outside this program's territory and the operator's decision (D26); nothing in this lane may reach for it. And the hold is an ARGUMENT, not a setting: the batch engine has no clock, so a pass given no hold is W9's pass exactly and E70's replay equivalence is untouched — proved by re-running it (44,724 of 44,724, 849 clusters) and by a replay variant that withholds every photograph at first claim, delivers them, expires the horizon, and reaches **the batch engine's state at both grains, 0 differing** (M118).
- **E94** **A gate that checked nothing must REFUSE, and one image's moved producer excuses ONE image.** W9g's gate counted breaches and nothing else, which is two vacuities and both were demonstrated: give every baseline listing a newer snapshot and every stored fact can then be broken with `checked` **0**, `breaches` **0**, `ok` true; delete every baseline listing and `absent` 120 passes just as quietly. Drift is monotone in export age (4 of 200 at three days), so the gate **self-weakened as the export aged** — precisely backwards. Three floors, all data and all refusing rather than passing: a minimum COMPARED count (`rt_parity_min_checked`, **15**) and a minimum SHARE of the slice (`rt_parity_min_checked_share`, **0.6**), because an absolute floor alone is defeated from both ends — a baseline smaller than 15 could never meet it and a 120-listing seed sample could meet it with 105 listings drifted or absent — with an EMPTY baseline requiring at least one comparison; and a ceiling on the share of phash-bearing images whose hash the frozen population cannot measure (`rt_parity_max_unknown_pop_share`, **0.15**, against a measured 4.0% at three days). And the population is compared **per IMAGE**: W9g skipped a listing's whole population check as soon as any image of it had been re-hashed, so one re-hashed frame excused its fourteen neighbours and a population decaying image by image ran green — the W9f defect in slow motion. The baseline therefore carries `[image_id, phash, pop]` per image (~54 KB for 120 listings, still one settings row), a frame whose own hash moved is excused and counted, and every other frame of that listing is still checked.
- **E95** **A frozen statistic has an expiry date, and the pass says how old it is every time.** E91 froze the pHash population and stated its staleness; nothing anywhere refused on it. The direction of the error is what makes that a defect rather than a shortcoming: a frozen population can only **UNDERCOUNT**, and an undercount is the ANTI-conservative direction — a frame that has spread since the export is still read as rare, E9 does not subtract it, and the pair gets image evidence the batch engine would deny it. So every pass reports the **EXPORT's** age (not the seed's: what is frozen is the cohort's statistics, and they go on ageing however recently the generation was cut from them), the calibration row's own age, and the population's **coverage** — the share of the photographs this pass actually scored that the frozen table could measure — and the lane REFUSES beyond `rt_calibration_max_age_days` (data, **14**). A baseline that cannot say when its cohort was exported is refused too, because a rail that cannot be evaluated is not a rail. **The refresh is one action, and what it preserves is stated:** a fresh export plus `rt_seed ... reseed=true`, which re-cuts the calibration, re-materialises `autodedup.phash_pop`, re-cuts the parity baseline and re-backfills the fingerprints. Operator verdicts and must-not-links are **generation-free** and survive untouched; `rt` pairs and clusters are rebuilt by the passes that follow, and the old rows are left where they are rather than rewritten, because they were decided under a scorer and on facts the new generation does not share.
- **E96** **An unmeasured population BANDS; it never merges.** The corollary E91(b) implies and W9h states as a rule, because it is what makes the staleness above tolerable rather than dangerous. A hash that appeared after the export cannot be counted without the export's own sequential scan — `images_phash_idx` is on `(sreality_id) where phash is not null`, there is no index from a hash to its carriers, D8 forbids adding one and schema `autodedup` mirrors nothing — so it reads **UNKNOWN**, `pop = None`. `pop_is_measured()` is then false, `catalog_ratio` is None, `catalog_ratio_max` is **ABSENT** on the pair, and `certificate_c` requires it PRESENT: the pair falls to the model score and **bands rather than merging on photo evidence nobody measured**. Measured at three days of export age: **115 of 2,847 images (4.0%)** read an unknown population and **54 of 400 pairs (13.5%)** lose `catalog_ratio_max` — the number E94's ceiling and E95's age rail are both proxies for, and the number a fresh export resets.
- **E110** **The honest clock's price is E84, and it is a price a SEALED read has measured — because the one number that refused the arm was a label the OPERATOR has now overturned.** E87 and E89 each took back a widening of `Settings.validate` on the same principle: a gate may only accept a price a sealed read has measured. W13 does not widen the gate, it RETIRES the demand. The arm in question is exactly W11's: the honest clock (E62), the E84 one-minute pair gap, **no E65 image floor, no E85 family guard, no E88 hold**, `w6_gold` unrefitted. W11's seal read it once and measured **347 of 406 sealed reliable labelled duplicates merged against g6's 300 — McNemar gained 47, lost 0, p ~ 7.1e-15 — at band 4,524 against 4,907** (M98). It met every clause of the promotion bar but one, and that one was a count of **1**: the sealed gold negative `522698 x 13221982`, whose dev-side twin is `555448 x 18626270`, both inside the two ceskereality Rezidence K Botiči serial-poster families F518656 and F521778 (D30 i). **On 2026-09-20 19:30 UTC the operator ruled BOTH pairs `same` in the validation UI** (`autodedup.verdicts`, pair grain, explicit, source `op:0871b32a9d227f60`; the W13 labels export carries them, M125). Operator labels outrank gold everywhere in this program, so the arm's reliable false-merge count is 0 at pair, block, family and cluster grain on every read taken since — and the sealed read that already exists becomes its measured price. E65's floor stays as live, tested, enforced settings rows (`certificate_b_min_images`, `certificate_b_min_matched_images`), it is simply no longer **required**: it withheld 89 % of the one-unit re-post chains' merges (M96) to guard against a hazard the operator has now said is not one. **What this acceptance rests on, and what revokes it, stated in the rule so nobody has to re-derive it: it rests on ONE operator ruling about ONE development** — two ceskereality families of one broker at K Botiči — **and it is revoked by an operator NEGATIVE inside a K-B family**, a pair the operator rules is two units that K-B certifies under the honest clock. Revoking it is a settings change, not a code change: a number back in `certificate_b_min_images` restores the W9 arm exactly. Two consequences of retiring the gate are recorded rather than hidden: the refuted rows under `settings/refuted/` **build again** (E87's convention keeps only its first half — `settings/` is what a scoring run may be pointed at, `refuted/` records a verdict), and the three refusals E83/E85/E88 now rest on their `off` defaults and their measurements alone, which is where E28's other refused rules have always rested.
- **E111** **A rule that names the event which revokes it must COUNT that event, not remember it.** E110 accepts the honest live-window clock on one operator ruling about one development and names what takes the acceptance back — an operator NEGATIVE inside a K-B family, a pair the operator rules is two units whose two adverts sit in one component of the pairs K-B certified under that clock. Left as prose, that condition expires silently: labels arrive every time the operator works the validation UI, and nobody re-derives a sentence in a rule index. So it is a check — `autodedup/revocation.py`, printed by `harness evaluate` on every run — and its three definitions are the ones already in the program rather than a second spelling of them. The **family** is `family.kb_families`' family, read off the CERTIFICATE and never off a zone or a merge edge (E85: a component read off its own verdict moves under it). The **pair** must have BOTH adverts inside ONE family: an operator negative between two chains, or between a family member and an outsider, is a separation the engine already makes and says nothing about K-B. The **tier** is the operator's alone — a gold `different` inside these families is exactly the reading E110 overturned (D31 vii: gold contradicts the operator on 5 of 10 same-family positives), so counting it would revoke the rule with the evidence the rule rejects; an operator `must_not_link` counts however it is spelled. The check REPORTS and never decides, because revoking E110 is a settings change and not a code change: a number back in `certificate_b_min_images` restores the W9 arm exactly. At promotion it reads **0 negatives over 196 operator-labelled pairs inside 173 K-B families** (M134).

---

## 2. Q1 — Duplicate definition and hard guards

**Definition (E1).** Two listings are duplicates iff they advertise the same physical unit under the same deal type — irrespective of portal, broker, advert age, price, or photo set.

**In scope:** re-listings across time (E6), same-portal same-broker re-posts (E7), active↔inactive (E8).

**Explicitly not duplicates:** two different units in the same building or development, *even sharing marketing photos, the same site plan, the same address, the same broker and boilerplate text*. This is the hard negative class and the engine is shaped around it.

**The hard guards, restated exactly** (E2, E3), with the tightenings the engine adds because it gates a merge rather than a candidate (E5), and the grain correction (E4).

One note on mechanics: a re-post that reuses the same `source_id_native` is already **one row** via `listings_source_native_uidx` (migration 091). Only new-id re-posts create duplicate rows, which is what the engine is for.

### How re-listings and re-posts are handled

These are mechanically the easiest pairs (byte-identical photos, near-identical text) and the most dangerous (a broker's template is reused across units). The engine therefore never accepts *template evidence* alone. A re-post merges on **unit-specific** evidence: identical or ±1 m² area, identical floor, interior-room photo matches (not exterior, not plan), rare-token overlap in the description. Certificate K-B (E24) handles the clean case without spending a judge; its zero-active-overlap clause is what separates a re-post from a development.

### How developer projects are kept apart — five independent rails

1. **Blocking never lets a shared-photo block explode into pairs** (E17): an exploded photo block is the catalogue detector, and membership becomes a negative feature.
2. **Catalog-photo subtraction before any image feature** (E9), with the leftover ratio fed back as a negative.
3. **Image evidence split by tag family** (E10) — "same building" and "same unit" are *different numbers*, so a shared plan can never be read as unit identity.
4. **The evidence-diversity gate** (E11): images alone can never merge.
5. **The four-way judge verdict** (E27): `same_building_different_unit` becomes a permanent must-not-link and is a far more informative negative label than plain "different", and the prompt is explicitly adversarial about Czech developer practice.

Plus two block-level priors: a block where ≥8 listings share one `broker_identity_id` with pairwise text containment ≥0.7 is flagged `project_suspected`, which raises `T_hi` inside the block and routes every borderline pair to vision; and the assembled negative-control cohort (§3) exists so this failure mode is *measured*, not assumed.

**Honest limit.** Shared photos + identical text + identical disposition + areas within 8% + an absent unit number is genuinely under-determined. If the trial shows this, the correct answer is to route the whole `project_suspected` block to propose-only. That costs recall, not precision.

---

## 3. Q2 — Trial region and sample

The corpus is all-time (822,599 listings; 612,076 reach a town), so town counts run ~2.2–2.7× their active counts. Praha alone is 79% of all country pairs even after a 119-quarter split, so it distorts every cost estimate and is excluded as the primary block.

**Ruling D1 fixes the cohort shape — four blocks, sized to budget, final pick after the census:**

1. **PRIMARY** — the top-ranked non-Praha district town, **2,000–3,500 all-time** listings. The regime a rollout would live in.
2. **SPARSE** — one small town, **500–700 all-time**. This block doubles as the **exhaustive O(n²) blocking-recall bound**: at ~600 listings that is ~180k pairs of pure CPU, $0 (the *labels* for it are budgeted; enumerating the pairs is free).
3. **DENSE** — one Praha `cast_obce_kod` quarter, **1,000–1,500 all-time**, chosen for new-build activity (developer stress). `cast_obce_kod` covers 85% of Praha vs `momc_kod`'s 24%.
4. **NEGATIVE CONTROL** — assembled, not geographic, **≤ 800 listings**: every listing in blocks 1–3 that either shares a `ruian_adm_kod` with another listing at a **different floor**, or carries a dHash appearing on **≥ 5 distinct listings**. Free to build from census probe B4. A mandatory evaluation stratum (E23).

Hard filters and the weighted score are specified in full in the CENSUS SPEC: ≥6 of 9 portals; all four `category_main` cells ≥30 rows; both deal types present; ≥70% of listings with a stored image and ≥90% of those with CLIP; size 1,500–5,000; not Praha as primary. **The operator picks the blocks off the census table; the program does not pick for them.**

**Expected pair volume.** The small-town pilot measured 3–11 candidates/listing with a single town+attribute rung; six probes roughly triples that. Expect 12–25 p50 in a district town, 30–60 p99 in the Praha quarter, capped at 60 (E17). At the cohort sizes D1 fixes, that is **~40,000–60,000 distinct trial pairs** (cohort × c / 2 — a pair is scored once, not once per side; the Appendix A convention) — a small artifact, iterable locally at zero cost.

**Iteration plan and what it costs.** The secretless shell is unblocked by **one export dispatch**: W1 ships the whole cohort as a gzipped Actions artifact (fingerprints, scrubbed descriptions, phash lists, snapshot price series, hashed broker keys, location fields, **and precomputed CLIP/pHash cross-evidence for every generated candidate pair**). After that, blocking, features, model fitting, calibration, clustering and evaluation all run **locally at $0**. Photos can be eyeballed locally through the public `GET /images/{storage_path}` → 302 presigned R2, no secrets needed.

Expect **6–10 offline iterations** (free) and **3 judge-prompt iterations** (paid). Paid sequence: I0 census $0 → I1 export $0 → I2 ground truth $32 → I3 prompt iteration $10 → I4 full trial pass + recall probes $60 → I5 shadow week $10 (only if W7 is approved). **~$102 committed of $200**, ~$112 if the W7 shadow week is approved (D2), and **every one of those iterations is a row in `autodedup.iterations`**, which is what the progress page renders (§12).

---

## 4. Q3 — Candidate generation (blocking)

O(block) per new listing (E13, E14), robust to the four structural holes: shared town-centroid pins, missing area, missing broker, missing disposition.

Each listing emits a small set of blocking keys into `autodedup.listing_fp` and `autodedup.image_band`. Retrieval is a lookup of that listing's keys, union of co-members, minus self, capped.

| probe | key | fires when | what it buys |
|---|---|---|---|
| **K1** block+attr | `(block_key, cat_group, category_type)` narrowed by equal `disposition` **or** `area_band ∈ {b−1,b,b+1}` | either attribute present | the workhorse |
| **K2** address | `(obec_kod, street_key, house_number_cp)` | both present (~17.5% corpus) | near-decisive; **rescues attribute-less rows** |
| **K3** broker | `(broker_key, cat_group, category_type, price_decile)` | broker resolved | same-broker re-posts **across town boundaries** |
| **K4** dHash bands | 4 × 16-bit bands of the anchor images' dHash | any phashed image | **location-free**; the only lane that works when every attribute changed |
| **K5** text bands | SimHash/MinHash bands over description shingles | description ≥200 chars | **location-free**; the bazos lifeline |
| **K6** foreign | `(country_code, cat_group, category_type, area_band)` | `country_status='foreign'` | the 45,619 abroad listings are an answer, not a loss |

`block_key` = `cast_obce_kod` in the split cities, else `obec_kod`; `cat_group` folds `{dum, komercni}` into one token so the sanctioned cross-type survives blocking. The **log-band trick** (`band = FLOOR(LN(area)/w)`, `w = −ln(1−t)`, join offsets {−1,0,+1}) is lifted verbatim from `toolkit/dedup_candidates_sql.py` as the *key derivation*, which turns a range tolerance into an equality lookup.

**K4/K5 are deliberately location-free, and that is the design's most important reach decision.** Only 10.2% of the corpus is geo-blockable and 17.5% carries a street key. The engine's reach must not be capped by the location program's reach.

**Anchor-image selection for K4** (`ad_anchor_images`, **3** per D8): the images with the highest *interior*-family tag score, falling back to sequence 0,1,2. Indexing by cover shot instead would bias the whole image lane toward facade and site imagery — exactly the catalogue failure mode. Catalog hashes (E9) are excluded from anchors. **D8 also rules out any DDL on `public.images` in this program** — no new shared-table index of any kind; the image lane runs on what already exists, and if that proves too slow the finding is reported, not patched with a hot-table index.

**The failure modes, answered**
- *Shared town-centroid pins* (122,920 listings on 4,206 pins, largest 1,029): a non-issue by construction — no probe keys on a coordinate (E16). Distance is a masked, uncertainty-normalised feature with a `pin_pop` discount.
- *Missing area* (all 138,997 bazos rows): K1's disposition arm still fires; K2/K4/K5 are unaffected. bazos's `postal_town` disagrees with the geo obec on 57% of rows, so **for bazos the photo and text probes outrank the town probe** — expressed as a data row in `autodedup.settings`, never a branch in shared code (CLAUDE.md rule 21).
- *Missing broker* (bazos, bezrealitky, maxima carry no broker row at all): K3 emits nothing; `broker_known` is a mask feature.
- *Missing disposition* (realitymix's 20,722 town-known attribute-less rows, all land): K1's area arm plus K2/K4/K5. For `pozemek`, area is the only identity attribute anyway.
- *remax*: reach is contradicted between two repo sources and is **re-measured in the census** (probe B2). If still unlocated, remax rides K3/K4/K5.

**Explosion control (E17).** An exploded `attr` block falls through to the next-most-specific key; an exploded *photo* block is retained as the catalogue detector. Fan-out fills in priority order `addr > phash > text > broker > attr1 > attr3`, so the cap discards only the weakest class.

**Expected pairs per listing: 12–25 p50, ≤60 p99.** At 25 candidates a `resolve()` pass is ~6 index probes plus ~25 feature computations — sub-second, which is what would make it a worker lane rather than a batch job.

---

## 5. Q4 — Pairwise features at zero marginal cost

One bulk fetch per block: the listing row (`scraper/db.py` `LISTING_COLUMNS`), the `listing_location` row, images (sequence, phash, storage_path, CLIP vector, tag), the last ~12 `listing_snapshots` price points, broker ids. Order versioned in `autodedup/features.py:FEATURE_ORDER`. All obey E12.

**Scope ruling (program lead).** The catalogue below is ~45 features. **W2 ships a core set of ~25** — the ones with a stated mechanism and no measurement dependency — and the set **grows only by error analysis in W4**: a feature is added because a named error class demands it, and the iteration that added it is recorded in `autodedup.iterations` with the metric delta it bought. Certificates K-A/K-B/K-C (E24) are in the core set.

**Attributes.** `area_rel_diff`, `area_exact`, `area_known_both`; `dispo_equal`; `floor_diff`, `floor_known_both`; `total_floors_equal`; `ownership_equal`, `building_type_equal`, `condition_equal`, `energy_equal`, `furnished_equal`, `subtype_equal`; boolean amenities (`has_lift`, `cellar`, `garage`, `terrace`, `parking_lots`) collapsed into **`attr_agreements`**, **`attr_contradictions`** (both known and different — the strongest single negative in the set) and **`attr_agreements_rare`** (each agreement weighted by inverse in-block frequency, so "both energy B" outscores "both has_lift"). `has_balcony`/`has_parking` are legacy conflated booleans and carry a reduced weight. `area_m2` only — the one headline area since location W17.

**The parcel, and the vocabulary the slots are read in (W4e, `FEATURE_VERSION` 3).** Error analysis put three residual merge-zone errors on a fact no slot compared — `estate_area` and `garden_area` are numeric and sit outside `ATTR_KEYS` — so the v3 tail appends **`plot_area_rel_diff`** and **`plot_area_exact`** (≤2 %), both ATTR, both present only when BOTH sides carry a *trusted* plot. The plot is `estate_area`, and on `pozemek` the headline area (measured identical on 177/177 rows carrying both); `garden_area` is never it (median 0.84 of the estate). Trust is gated **two ways**, because the truncating parsers fail in two shapes (M24). *Per (source, carrier)*, where the population convicts the whole column: five portals parse areas with a regex that has no thousands separator, so ceskereality reads "5 870 m²" as **870** and 0 of its 173 estate values reaches 1,000 where sreality and idnes reach it on 43–45 %. *Per value*, where it does not: bazos is the largest headline carrier the code reads (40 of 70 rows) and does reach 1,000 on 13 of them, so only the listing's own advert can convict the number — a stored plot that is a three-digit tail of a space-grouped number in its own description ("11 197 m²" stored as 197) is dropped, a test that flags 6 of bazos' 40 and **0 of the 431 values on the four clean carriers**. A plot below 10 m² is a data-entry artefact (36 rows carry `estate_area = 1`) and never reaches the comparison; a pair whose smaller plot is exactly the last three digits of the larger is read as ABSENT rather than as a contradiction. `plot_area_exact` is ATTR corroboration, but on **thin and unseparated** evidence: 40 of 42 judged duplicates carrying two trusted plots agree within 2 %, against 3 of 9 non-duplicates — the whole labelled negative set that carries them. Tightening the threshold and gating on plot rarity were both measured and neither separates (the false fires are exact matches on a modal parcel: 740 m² is the median `dum` estate on both sreality and idnes). The rule is kept because it decides nothing today (stripping it moves 0 pairs between zones) and is owed a labelled draw before the refit leans on it. Alongside it, **attribute comparison now runs on canonical values** (`normalize.canonical_attr`): portal synonyms fold onto one token (`ve_vystavbe_(hruba_stavba)` = `ve_vystavbe`, `zdeny` = `cihla`, `velmi_dobry` = `dobry`, `ano` = `castecne`) and "other"/"unspecified" values (`jina`, 123 ceskereality rows) read as absent — each fold measured to raise the contradiction ratio between judged duplicates and non-duplicates (M22). A `false` in a boolean slot from a portal that only ever publishes the positive is a parser default, not a fact, and is dropped (`FALSE_BY_OMISSION`, M23); a portal that *does* publish the negative keeps contradicting.

**Price and price history.** `price_last_ratio`, `ppm2_rel_diff`, `price_magnitude_class_equal` (sale-scale vs rent-scale — the fallback when `category_type` is NULL), and the strong one: **`price_path_event_match`** (E19) plus `price_path_shift_ratio` for the re-listing shape.

**Text (stdlib only).** Reuses `location_data/resolver/normalize.py` (`deaccent`, `normalize_match_key`). `jaccard_5shingle`; **`containment_max`** (catches truncated re-posts, which plain Jaccard misses); `tfidf_cos` over the block corpus; `desc_simhash_hamming`; `len_ratio`; and the discriminators (E20) **`rare_token_overlap`** (in-block df ≤2: street names, unit codes, project names) and **`numeric_fact_overlap`** / **`numeral_conflict`** over `(value, unit)` pairs (m², Kč, NP/patro, unit numbers). Conflicting numerals in the same slot type is itself a reject condition. No MinHash storage beyond the blocking sketch — exact Jaccard is computed on the fly for ≤60 candidates.

**Broker.** `same_broker_identity`, `same_firm`, `same_phone_norm`, `same_email_domain`, `broker_known_both`, `firm_is_franchise` (a franchise email is not an identity), `contact_rarity` (a phone/email with corpus df ≥50 — mmreality's single switchboard across 12 broker ids — contributes nothing).

**Location** — read only from `listing_location` (location lives ONLY there since migration 508). `same_ruian_adm_kod` (address-point identity, near-decisive: sreality 26.9%, bezrealitky 60.7%, idnes 1.4%, bazos 0%); `same_street_key`, `same_house_number_cp`, `same_psc`, `same_cast_obce`; `dist_m` emitted **only** when both pins clear the precision gate, else NULL with an explicit `pin_quality` indicator; **`dist_norm = dist_m / (r_a + r_b + 25)`** so 400 m between two address points is damning and 400 m between two municipal pins is noise; `granularity_rank_min` via `location_granularity_rank` (never the enum's order); `same_exact_pin` paired with `pin_pop`.

**Images** — all computed **after** catalog subtraction (E9). `phash_tight` (Hamming ≤6, `bit_count((a # b)::bit(64))` — with the cast; `bit_count` is not defined for bigint), `phash_loose` (≤11), **`phash_match_ratio` = matches / min(n_a, n_b)** (the ratio carries more signal than the count); `clip_max_cos`, `clip_mean_top3_cos` (11.3M vectors, no ANN index needed at ≤60 candidates × ≤15 images, and **zero storage** — computed lazily per pair); **`seq_monotone_ratio`** (matched pairs preserving gallery order — a re-post keeps order, a catalogue reuse does not); the **three tag-family ratios** (E10); `catalog_ratio`; `shared_photo_df_max`; `n_images_min`.

**Temporal.** `gap_days`, `overlap_days`, `both_active`, `same_source`, and `source_pair` one-hot over the 45 unordered source pairs, which lets the model learn per-portal-pair priors.

---

## 6. Q5 — Decision model

**Layer 1 — the rule floor** (`autodedup/guards.py` + `decide.py`). Pure Python, no training, absolute authority, evaluated first and never overridden. It (a) vetoes on E2–E5 **and on E61's conflicting unit designator**, (b) auto-rejects on `attr_contradictions ≥ 3` or `numeral_conflict`, (c) auto-accepts the certificates — E24's three, plus **E60's K-R, which is read before them** — which also seed the bootstrap positive labels before any judge has run.

**Layer 2 — a calibrated stdlib logistic regression** (E21), over the feature set plus a handful of hand-specified interaction terms encoding the domain physics a linear model cannot see alone: `interior_match_ratio × (1 − shared_photo_block)`, `rare_token_overlap × same_source`, `dist_m × pin_quality_min`, `area_exact × dispo_equal`, `price_path_event_match × gap_days_bucket`, `catalog_ratio × phash_match_ratio`.

Why learned rather than hand-tuned: the interaction between *missingness patterns* and *source pair* is a surface nobody will tune by hand, and it is exactly where bazos, remax and realitymix live. But the model **starts hand-initialised from priors** so W2 can run before any labels exist, and those hand weights remain the fallback if a fit degrades. If measured AUC lands below 0.985, the fallback is ~200 lines of stdlib gradient-boosted decision stumps, still interpretable.

**A fitted model is checked against the CODE at load, not only against itself (W4e).** The `provenance.feature_version` stamp is a digest of the feature order the artifact carries, so comparing it to the artifact is tautological: a model fitted before a feature landed passes the stamp and then scores the new feature at zero, silently. `LogisticModel.check_feature_order` supplies the other half — `score` looks weights up by NAME, so the invariant is membership: a feature name the code no longer defines is a **load-time error**, and a name in `features.FEATURE_ORDER` the model does not carry is the **refit debt** and warns naming every one. Only a stamped (i.e. fitted) artifact is checked; a hand-built model over a deliberate subset is exempt. `w4_gold` carries 45 of the current 47 names and warns about `plot_area_rel_diff` and `plot_area_exact` on every load until it is refitted.

**Three zones** (E22) and **the diversity gate** (E11) — a pair with only one evidence family, however high its score, is forced into the band.

**Calibration and thresholds.** On the sealed, cluster-split holdout: precision(t) and recall(t) with **Clopper–Pearson** exact intervals, **per stratum** (E23/D10). `T_hi` = the smallest t whose **Wilson 95% lower bound on precision ≥ 0.99 with point ≥ 0.995, and no stratum below 0.97** (D3). `T_lo` = the largest t at which cumulative missed positives below it are ≤3% of all positives, **Horvitz–Thompson-weighted** by the known per-stratum sampling rates. `b = P(T_lo < p < T_hi)` is then read off and is the budget dial (E25). Thresholds live on the `models` row, so a threshold change *is* a model version change.

**The sample-size arithmetic, stated because it is where evaluation plans usually break.** A Wilson 95% lower bound of 0.99 on a perfect sample needs **n ≥ 380**; with one error, ~650. A 150-pair stratum can never clear a 0.99 gate — its best possible lower bound is 0.975. The precision sample is therefore **400 gold-judged auto-merge pairs minimum** (D3).

**Why 99.5% precision would justify autonomy — and what it does not.** A false merge fuses two properties and corrupts the price history the operator is buying. It is recoverable: `unmerge_group` replays the ledger deterministically, and every autonomous merge would be a **single-child** `merge_group_id` (E38), so reverting a whole generation is linear. At ~30,000 expected corpus-wide merges, 99.5% would leave ~150 bad merges — all surfaced in the residual/groups UI and all revertible. **Reversibility is what buys the threshold, not the threshold itself.** In this program nothing is applied at all (D4), so nothing can be reverted either: reversibility stays an **argued** claim here — argued from `unmerge_group`'s deterministic replay and from the single-child group shape (E38) — and is carried into W6's decision package **with that caveat stated**. Measuring it means applying and undoing real merges through the production chokepoint, which this program may not do; that rehearsal is Appendix A5 and needs its own operator decision.

---

## 7. Q6 — LLM judge

Full specification is delivered separately as the **JUDGE SPEC**. In outline: pre-flight obligations (the `called_for` migration and `PRICES` rows before any calling code; the `--max-usd` "done not drew" assertion; smoke runs for the never-exercised vision-in-batch and Qwen paths); T1 `gpt-5.6-luna` text on 100% of band pairs at **$0.00086/pair batched**, T2 `gpt-5-mini` vision on ~30% at **$0.00284/pair batched**, blended **$0.0017 per banded pair**; a three-vote gold judge at ~$0.018/pair; the four-way forced-tool verdict with a required `unit_discriminator` (E27); the PII rule (E28); the three separately-reported agreement numbers with **gold-vs-operator below 0.95 stopping the program** (D6).

Two things worth repeating here because they change the cost model. First, `gpt-5-mini` and `gpt-5.6-luna` are **reasoning models**: this design budgets 1,200–1,500 output tokens per verdict, roughly double what a naive count gives, and W3 replaces the estimate with the measured `llm_calls` average before any threshold is fixed (E32). Second, `COMPARISON_MAX_EDGE = 768` at q80 is the single biggest cost lever in the whole program — roughly one third the vision tokens of the 1568 tier.

**Spend gate (D2).** A lane costing **under $10 runs autonomously**; **$25 is the hard per-run cap** wired into `--max-usd`; **$200 is the program total**. Every paid lane asserts the batch read *done*, not merely *drew*, and writes its measured spend into the `autodedup.iterations` row for that iteration so the progress page shows real dollars, never forecasts.

---

## 8. Q7 — Clustering

Constrained union-find with must-not-link (E33), invariants (E34), immutable identity and medoid attachment (E36), and no autonomous bridging (E37) — narrowed by **E57**, which re-offers a refused bridge once and applies it only when the merged member set satisfies every invariant and the edge is a certificate or near-certain.

Every refused union writes an `autodedup.cluster_conflicts` row with the offending invariant — these are the **highest-value items in the validation UI**, because a conflict means the engine found strong evidence in both directions.

**What the cluster row stores** (`autodedup.clusters`): `cluster_key`, `generation`, `size`, `block_key`, `cat_group`, `category_main`, `category_type`, `area_min/max`, `sources[]`, `medoid_listing_id`, `min_edge_score` (the weakest accepted edge — the UI's default sort, because that is where errors live), `mean_edge_score`, `n_judged_edges`, `n_certificate_edges`, `evidence_families` (bitmask), `max_gap_days`, `shared_photo_warning`, `status`, `property_id`, `model_version`, `feature_version`, timestamps. Members live in `cluster_members` with `joined_via_lo/hi`.

**Fragmentation is a gate, not just a metric.** Under E6 a property may carry five or more listings across years. Splitting one true property into three clusters is invisible both to pairwise precision *and* to a residual scan that only surfaces pairs above a score floor — so the operator's UI would show a clean result while the price history the program exists to build stays fragmented. §9 therefore gates on cluster **recall** (mean clusters per true property ≤ 1.15 on gold-judged properties), not only purity.

---

## 9. Q8 — Evaluation protocol

**Labels** (`autodedup.labels`), all fresh and all built inside `autodedup.*` (D7 — nothing is imported from any prior dedup effort, and `property_merge_events` is not a label source): operator verdicts (weight 1.0, the oracle), gold LLM three-vote (0.6), cheap LLM (0.3), rule-certain certificates (bootstrap). Split **60/20/20 by cluster, never by pair** — pairs inside one cluster are not independent and a pair-level split leaks.

**AN IMPLIED LABEL COMES OUT OF THE RULING, NOT OUT OF THE CURRENT CLUSTERING (E58).** Confirming a group asserts that every pair inside it is one property — inside **that** group, the set of adverts the operator was looking at, which `verdicts.member_ids` now records. Before it did, the labels lane and the agreement read expanded a confirmation over `cluster_members` at read time, so a later pass that grew or absorbed the key silently changed what an old label asserted: promoting g5 moved 21 of the operator's 224 confirmations that way and then erased g4's clusters entirely, leaving `mode=labels generation=g4` unable to reproduce itself at all. Both reads now expand `member_ids`; a legacy row with none falls back to the members of its OWN generation, and only where the key belongs to one pass. **"Does this ruling apply here?" is answered on the SET by all three readers** — the Groups page, the agreement read and the labels lane: after 538 every backfilled ruling reads `g4`, so a generation-string test alone would have exported zero implied labels for g5 while the UI showed 203 of those rulings applying to g5 groups that never moved. The string arm survives as a union, not as the test — it is what keeps a pass's own rulings exportable while that pass's clusters are missing from the store. The engine view beside a label (`ENGINE_PAIRS_SQL`) is likewise scoped by the pair's own `generation` column rather than by the `(model_version, feature_version)` its clusters happened to carry — a derivation that broke the moment those clusters were re-stamped away. The seeded `sample_rank` an implied label carries is still a property of the generation's live cluster set, so reproducing a g4 sample needs g4's clustering back (a re-run of the score lane at g4's settings), not just the verdicts.

**Metrics and pass criteria**

| # | metric | how | pass |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Pairwise precision** at auto-merge | 400 gold-judged auto-merge pairs, Clopper–Pearson/Wilson 95% | **LB ≥ 0.99 on n ≥ 380, point ≥ 0.995, no stratum < 0.97** (D3), no systematic error class ≥3 occurrences |
| 2 | **Pairwise recall** | Horvitz–Thompson-weighted over score-decile strata with known sampling rates; bootstrap CI over 2,000 resamples | ≥ 0.85 at auto-merge, ≥ 0.95 including the band |
| 3 | **The recall probe** — the honest attack on "we cannot see the positives we threw away" | 300 sampled listings: uncapped retrieval (every probe, no `max_block_size`) **plus** brute-force in-block CLIP top-50; cheap-judge everything the engine rejected (~9,000 pairs), gold-judge whatever the cheap judge calls a duplicate (~250) | missed-duplicate rate ≤ 2% of listings |
| 4 | **Blocking-recall bound** | one exhaustive O(n²) pass over the ~600-listing sparse block (~180k pairs, pure CPU, $0), labels budgeted | reported, not gated — it bounds what the probes miss |
| 5 | **Cluster purity** | 100 sampled clusters, every internal pair judged | ≥ 0.98 |
| 6 | **Cluster recall / fragmentation** | mean clusters per gold-judged true property | ≤ 1.15 |
| 7 | **Residual-duplicate rate** | the operator scrolls the residual view for 30 minutes | < 5 true duplicates per 100 properties reviewed |
| 8 | **Judge fidelity** | cheap-vs-gold ≥ 0.93; **gold-vs-operator ≥ 0.95 on a 200-pair operator session run through the validation UI, or the program stops** (D6); gold unanimity ≥ 0.92 | as stated |
| 9 | **Cost per decided listing** | read from `llm_calls` by `called_for` and model (E32) | projected monthly ≤ $150 |

**Metric 10 (revert wall-clock) has moved to Appendix A5 and is not a gate of this program.** Timing a revert means applying 500 real merges through `merge_properties` and undoing them — production writes to `properties`, `listings.property_id`, `property_merge_events` and operator state, which D4 forbids here. Reversibility therefore stays the argued claim of §6, and W6's decision package says so in those words.

**Sample sizes.** 4,000 cheap bootstrap labels; 1,200 gold; 400 precision; ~9,250 recall probe; 200 operator-anchored (the D6 session); 100 clusters. Strata: auto-merge / band / near-reject / far-reject / same-portal / cross-portal / bazos / remax / land+commercial / exploded-photo-block / **negative control**.

**How the D6 agreement number is computed (E55), and by what.** It is read live off the store by `GET /autodedup/agreement` and shown on the progress page as **"Shoda operátor × soudce"**, rather than computed once by a lane at the end of a wave. The comparison is **binary — one property, or not** — because the two vocabularies are not the same vocabulary: the operator has five verdicts (migration 532), the judge four, and only that distinction is common to both. `same` ↔ `same_property` is "one"; `different` / `same_building_different_unit` / `same_project_different_unit` ↔ `different_property` / `same_building_different_unit` is "not one". The **operator's label set is two things unioned**: explicit pair verdicts, and the pairs **IMPLIED** by a cluster verdict of `same` (confirming a group is a statement about every pair inside it — 103 confirmed groups carry far more pair-grade evidence than the handful of pairs ruled on one at a time). An explicit verdict **wins** over an implied one, `unsure` is not a label, and only `same` clusters imply anything: a rejected group says the members are not *all* one property, never which pair inside it was wrong. The implied expansion is **bounded** (clusters above 12 members are not expanded — n members imply n(n−1)/2 pairs — and the number of confirmed groups skipped is reported, not dropped silently). The **judge's** label is the best tier per pair, **gold > vision > text**, with `oss` excluded entirely and `insufficient_evidence` excluded from the agreement and **counted separately**. Each stratum reports n, agreement and a **Wilson 95%** interval (computed in `autodedup/agreement.py`, in Python, where it can be checked by hand), plus the two directions of disagreement kept apart — judge said `different` where the operator said `same`, and the reverse — and the explicit/implied split of the labels, so the number can never quietly become a number about groups. The page prints the D6 bar beside it and how far the session is from the ~200 gold-labelled pairs the gate names; below that bar it says the sample is not yet enough to decide, rather than painting a verdict on an unfinished number.

**Feedback loop.** Operator verdicts land in `autodedup.verdicts` and, for negatives, in `must_not_link`. They override gold labels, become weight-1.0 training rows, and would drive **the latch** (E42) on any future write path. A refit is triggered manually, never automatically, and a new model must beat the incumbent **on the sealed test split** before `activate` flips the active flag.

**The verdict vocabulary is five values** (migration 528, widened by **532**): `same` · `different` · `same_building_different_unit` · `same_project_different_unit` (E49) · `unsure`. The last four-to-five widening came straight off the review queue: a proposed group held one advert from a *different building of the same development*, two adverts for *one* unit, and two more for *two further units of that development*. `different` throws the project away and `same_building_different_unit` asserts a building those adverts do not share — so the operator had no way to record what they had actually established. **Three of the five are negative** and each writes the permanent `must_not_link`; `same` retracts the operator's own row; `unsure` writes neither.

**The split verdict** — `POST /autodedup/verdict/split` `{cluster_key, generation, units:[{listing_id, unit}], relation, relations?, confirm_retract?, note?}` — is how a whole group is ruled on unit by unit, because a group is frequently not one answer. The operator labels every member with a unit (a letter), and the route writes, **in one transaction**:

- for each member pair **inside one unit**: a pair verdict `same`, plus a `must_not_link` **retraction** (the correction half of the loop — a veto the operator has taken back must stop vetoing);
- for each member pair **across two units**: a pair verdict equal to the relation named for *those two units* (E51 — one of `same_building_different_unit` · `same_project_different_unit` · `different`; `same` is not offered, since two different units are never one property), plus a permanent `must_not_link` with reason `operator split: <relation>`;
- the **cluster** verdict: `same` when one unit was used, otherwise the **weakest** relation the split used, carrying the assignment (`A: 94020,140903 | B: 94492 | C: 268009`) as its note.

**The candidate split** — `POST /autodedup/verdict/candidate-split` `{candidate_key, generation, units:[{listing_id, unit}], relation, relations?, confirm_retract?, note?}` — is the same ruling over a **candidate group** (E56), the packing of residual pairs the `/autodedup/residual` groups view serves. It writes, in ONE transaction and through the same statements: a `same` pair verdict plus a must-not-link **retraction** for each member pair inside one unit, and the named relation plus a permanent `must_not_link` for each pair across two units. Three differences from the cluster split, all of them consequences of there being **no cluster row**:

- **no cluster verdict is written** — a candidate group is a packing of this generation's residual pairs, not a proposal the engine made, so there is nothing to confirm or reject; the ruling IS its pair rows, which is also how the card reads itself back (E50);
- **a lock is never written through** — a member pair whose two adverts belong to the SAME already-merged group is skipped entirely (reported as `n_pairs_locked`), because that pair is the Groups page's ruling; and an assignment that gives two adverts of one lock different letters is a **400** that names the group and says where to split it;
- **no reason chips** — the split stamps its chips on the cluster row, and there is none here; stamping the fan-out instead would post one reason row per pair from a single click and the §9 histogram would measure card size rather than what the operator saw. A non-empty `reasons` is a 400, not a silent drop; the free-text note rides on every pair row in front of the assignment summary.

Everything else is unchanged and deliberately so: the assignment must name every advert of the card exactly once (400), the card must exist in the named generation (404, and a generation nothing clustered is a 404 before anything is packed), the pair need not exist in `autodedup.pairs`, and the **409 confirmation** of E52 applies as written. The verdicts it writes are ordinary `kind='pair'` rows, so they reach `AGREEMENT_PAIRS_SQL`'s explicit operator set exactly as a pair ruled one at a time does — which is what makes a card ruled here count towards the D6 gate.

**A split that takes a veto back asks first** (E52): when a same-unit pair already carries a *negative* verdict from the same operator, the route answers **409** naming those pairs and writes nothing until the call repeats with `confirm_retract`. The assignment of a group the operator has already split is read back off the stored pair verdicts (E50), so the page they confirm from is the ruling that exists rather than a blank one.

**The reason vocabulary** (migration 533, `autodedup.verdicts.reasons text[]`). Every verdict control — pair, cluster and split — carries structured **reason chips** plus the free-text note that has been on the table since 528. This is the feedback loop's missing half: the verdict says WHAT the operator decided, the reasons say **what they saw**. A chip like `floor_plan_differs` on a pair the engine merged names a discriminator the feature set does not have, and the per-code histogram (`/autodedup/stats` → `engine.verdict_reasons`, pair and cluster grain kept apart) is directly comparable with the judge's `unit_discriminator` — the same question, asked of the model and of the human. It is read on the progress page as **"Důvody operátora"**.

The codes, with the operator's own labels: `floor_plan_differs` (Jiný půdorys) · `unit_number` (Číslo jednotky) · `kitchen_or_bathroom_differs` (Jiná kuchyň nebo koupelna) · `floor_differs` (Jiné podlaží) · `area_differs` (Jiná výměra) · `price_history` (Cenová historie sedí) · `description` (Popis) · `broker` (Stejný makléř) · `identical_photos` (Stejné fotky) · `catalogue_photos_only` (Jen katalogové fotky) · `relisted_after_gap` (Znovu inzerováno po pauze) · `same_project` (Stejný projekt) · `other` (Jiné).

Mechanics, in one place: the registry lives in `autodedup/verdict_reasons.py` and is **served** at `GET /autodedup/verdict-reasons` so the SPA hard-codes none of it (a copied list is a second vocabulary, and it drifts the first review session that names a new shape). The column carries **no CHECK constraint** — unlike 532's `verdict` domain — because the admin API is the only writer and validates every code (unknown ⇒ **400**), so growing the list is one line of Python and no migration. Codes are **de-duplicated, order preserved**; the note stays optional. A **split** stamps its reasons on the **cluster row only**, never on the pairwise fan-out: a split is one ruling, per-pair reasons would claim the operator said something about each edge that they never said, and — the reason it matters to the readout — one click on a 6-member group would otherwise post 15 pair rows, so the pair histogram would measure cluster size instead of evidence and stop being comparable with the judge's per-pair `unit_discriminator`. The fan-out rows keep their machine note (`operator split: …`). The cluster note still leads with the operator's words and then the assignment string. Re-deciding a verdict **upserts** reasons and note with it; editing only the annotation re-posts the stored verdict through the same endpoint. A store that predates 533 answers **503 naming 533** on a write (`UndefinedColumn`, distinct from 532's rejected `CheckViolation` value); a READ degrades instead — the stats histogram comes back empty, and the four review statements that select `v.reasons` (groups, group detail, residual, pair) answer `store_ready: false` like a missing relation, since `store_ready` probes the RELATION and cannot see a missing column. In the SPA, a card that shows both the split's picker and the cluster verdict's names them apart (**„důvod rozdělení"** vs **„důvod verdiktu"**): two identical toggles over two different drafts silently drop whichever set was not the one saved.

**Cluster MEMBERSHIP is the validation, never `autodedup.pairs`.** A cluster is the union of the edges the engine accepted, so two members can sit in one cluster with no scored edge between them at all; requiring a pair row would refuse exactly the rulings a five-member group needs. The assignment must therefore name every member of the cluster exactly once and nobody else (400), the cluster must exist in the named generation (404), and a store that predates migration 532 answers **503 naming the migration** rather than 500ing on the CHECK violation.

---

## 10. Q9 — Cost model at full scale

**Governing equation (E25):** `monthly = L × c × b × $0.0017`.

**L, measured not guessed.** The doc-sourced figure is 39,794 listings first seen in 7 days ≈ 5,685/day ≈ **170,000/month**. The census re-measures it (probe B5) before any threshold is fixed. `c = 20` candidates/listing.

| band `b` | monthly | per listing |
|---|---|---|
| 0.005 | **$29** | $0.00017 |
| 0.010 | **$58** | $0.00034 |
| 0.015 | **$87** | $0.00051 |
| 0.020 | **$116** | $0.00068 |
| 0.026 | $150 | $0.00088 — the ceiling |

`ad_band_budget` ships at **0.015 ≈ $87/month**, with `T_lo` auto-raising to fit and the sacrificed recall printed. Everything outside the band costs **$0**. This is the steady-state figure a *future* always-on lane would carry; in this program the only spend is the trial's own iterations (§14).

**Per-listing marginal cost** for blocking, features, scoring and clustering: pure CPU over already-paid substrate. **$0.**

**Corpus backfill is explicitly out of scope for this program (D9).** The arithmetic is retained in Appendix A so the rollout conversation has a number to start from, but nothing in W0–W7 backfills anything.

**Storage** (Supabase, outside the LLM budget — see the schema migration's sizing block): trial cohort well under 60 MB. The full-corpus projection is `listing_fp` 250 MB, `image_band` 660 MB (the largest line; `ad_anchor_images` 3→2 would take it to ~440 MB at a recall cost), `phash_pop` 30 MB, `pairs` 200 MB (only `score ≥ 0.02` persists), everything else ~100 MB — **~1.2 GB** — and that projection only becomes real if a backfill is later approved. No multi-GB pair table: rejected pairs are re-derivable from the block index and are counted, not stored.

**R2 Class-B reads.** The 10M/month free tier is per account and already consumed by production `/images/` redirects. The trial's vision judging adds a five-figure number of GetObjects at $0.36/M overage — pennies, but it belongs in the table because it lands on a line the $200 does not cover.

**GPU: $0.** CLIP (11.3M vectors) and dHash exist corpus-wide; cosines over ≤60 candidates need no ANN index. DINOv3 is a non-asset: migration 480 applied with **0 rows**, and `data/dinov3_config.json` still has null `resolution`/`preprocessing`/`dtype` so the loader deliberately refuses. **$20 is reserved** for a single RTX 3090 bake-off ($0.22/h) *only if* W4's error analysis proves image confusion is the dominant residual error class.

---

## 11. Q10 — Realtime integration (designed; not built in this program unless approved)

W7 ships this section as a **design-only document** by default; building the lane requires explicit operator approval (§14).

**The lane.** One tuple appended to the `lanes` list in `scraper/realtime_worker.py`, following the three-piece contract exactly as `location_resolve` does:

```python
("autodedup", lambda: _lane_loop(
    "autodedup", stop_event, _read_autodedup_interval,
    lambda: _autodedup_pass(stop_event, state), state,
    default_interval=0)),
```

`default_interval=0` is the **fail-safe, not a default**: `_lane_loop` keeps this value when the `app_settings` read *raises*, and the flag lives inside the read that just failed — a positive fallback would turn a dark lane on for one pass on any pooler blip, which is exactly when it must not run. The reader returns 0 unless `autodedup_enabled` is true. `_record_pass(state, "autodedup", last)` puts it in `worker_heartbeats` and on the Health page for free; `check_worker_lane_stall` reads `in_flight_s` only, so an idle lane never false-alarms. A pass is bounded by `LANE_PASS_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 1800` and `_supervised` restarts it after 30 s if it crashes.

**The queue.** `autodedup.resolve_queue`, drained `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (the `listing_detail_queue` idiom, CLAUDE.md rule 19). Two producers: a one-line enqueue beside the existing `dirty_properties` enqueues in `scraper/db.py` (fired on `new`, and on `updated` only when a **matching-relevant** column moved — price, area, disposition, floor, description, image set); and a backstop sweep over `listing_fp` rows whose `fp_version` is stale. `listing_snapshots` is an append-on-content-change feed (CLAUDE.md rules 2 and 8), so this needs no change to any scraper write path beyond the enqueue line.

**Per-listing pass** (target p95 < 60 s from enqueue): build/refresh `listing_fp` + `image_band` (delete-then-insert by `listing_id`, idempotent) → probe K1–K6 → bulk-fetch the block → features → rule floor → model → zone. Band pairs go to `autodedup.judge_queue`; the worker would drain them **synchronously** with a pre-flight budget check (the 24 h batch window plus GitHub cron throttle — a `*/30` lane has measured 80–256 minute gaps — would make "realtime" a fiction), while a dispatched Actions lane uses Batch at half price.

**Mutual exclusion:** lease-row CAS (`location_data/resolver/lease.py` pattern), **never** `pg_advisory_lock` — session locks strand over the transaction pooler. The same lease lets the worker and a dispatched Actions run share the code without overlap.

**Idempotency.** `listing_fp` keyed on `listing_id`; `pairs` on `(listing_lo, listing_hi)` with `lo < hi` CHECK; `image_band` on `(band_no, band_val, listing_id)`. All writes `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`. Re-running `resolve()` on the same listing, or re-running a whole generation, is safe. Both sides are additionally resolved through `resolve_active_property_ids` (`toolkit/property_identity.py`, which follows `merged_into`) before a pair is proposed: if they already share an active property the decision is a recorded **no-op** and never reaches `/autodedup/groups` — in shadow mode that is what keeps already-merged pairs out of the operator's review queue.

**The merge write — Appendix A material, never executed in this program.** Survivor = the property of the listing with the oldest `first_seen_at`, matching `api/property_merge.py`'s convention.

```python
group = str(uuid.uuid4())
store.record_intent(conn, lo, hi, group)          # E40: recorded BEFORE the call
res = merge_properties(conn, survivor_id=surv, retired_id=ret,
        reason=f"autodedup:{model_version}:{lo}-{hi}", source="auto",
        confidence=score, merge_group_id=group,
        markers={"engine": "autodedup", "generation": GENERATION,
                 "model_version": MV, "feature_version": FV,
                 "cluster_key": ck, "families": fams,
                 "gap_days": gap, "judge_llm_call_id": cid})
store.stamp_generation(conn, group)               # write-only touch of property_merge_events
store.enqueue_dirty(conn, surv)
```

`merge_properties` takes `merge_group_id` as a real keyword (verified), re-enforces E2/E3 at the chokepoint and raises `MergeError` — the last rail, never bypassed, with the policy at E41. Note that `recompute_one`, `recompute_mf_one` and `sync_browse_list` run **inline per merge**, which is why a bulk revert is a bulk read-model rebuild of unmeasured duration — the reason the rehearsal is gated as Appendix A5 rather than run here.

**Kill switches, escalating:** `autodedup_write_enabled=false` (score and store, write nothing — **the whole program's mode**, E39/D4) → `autodedup_enabled` interval 0 (lane dark) → the merges-per-hour latch (E42) → the operator-error-rate latch that a future write path would add (§9's feedback loop: operator verdicts marking applied merges wrong flip `autodedup_write_enabled` false above a rolling error rate the rollout decision sets). The first of these is the whole program's mode; the rest are Appendix A material.

**Unmerge safety.** `scripts/autodedup_revert.py --generation <g> | --since <ts> | --merge-group <id>` walks `autodedup.merges` newest-first and calls `unmerge_group`. Because every autonomous group has exactly one child, the revert is linear and conflict-free. **It is never exercised in this program** — no merge is applied, so none can be reverted (D4). The rehearsal that would time it is Appendix A5.

---

## 11a. Q10b — The real-time SHADOW lane, built (W9), hardened (W9b), scoped (W9c), railed (W9d), costed (W9e)

§11 designed a worker lane and left it unbuilt. W9 built the path itself — **the same decisions, made one arrival at a time** — as a dark, $0, shadow-only lane, and proved by replay that it reaches the cohort pass's state.
